Final Review Checklist
I can name and explain the five mother sauces.
I can explain stock making without saying "boil hard".
I can describe Caesar salad and vinaigrette clearly.
I can explain poached eggs, scrambled eggs and Eggs Benedict.
I know vegetable cuts and can link them to dishes.
I can explain soup types and pumpkin soup production.
I can answer all questions in 45-60 seconds using real examples.

Extended Interview Practice Cards
Use these additional cards for speaking practice. They are shorter than the main interview cards, so you can practise fast recall before the assessment.
Practice Card 1: How do you boil eggs for service?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
I place eggs in water, bring them to the required cooking point, then cool them quickly if they are for later use. I label and store them in the fridge according to our procedure.
Tôi cho trứng vào nước, luộc đến mức chín yêu cầu, sau đó làm nguội nhanh nếu dùng sau. Tôi ghi nhãn và bảo quản trong tủ mát theo quy trình.
Key words: boil, cool quickly, label, refrigerate
Assessor tip: Mention storage after cooking, not only the boiling step.
Practice Card 2: How do you make an omelette?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
I whisk eggs, season lightly, heat the pan, add a little butter or oil and cook the eggs gently. I add fillings if required, fold the omelette neatly and serve it hot.
Tôi đánh trứng, nêm nhẹ, làm nóng chảo, thêm bơ hoặc dầu và nấu nhẹ. Tôi thêm nhân nếu cần, gấp omelette gọn và phục vụ nóng.
Key words: whisk, season, fold, serve hot
Assessor tip: Talk about low to medium heat to avoid browning too much.
Practice Card 3: What can go wrong when cooking eggs?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
Eggs can be overcooked, dry, rubbery or unsafe if handled poorly. I control heat, cook to the right texture and avoid cross contamination.
Trứng có thể bị quá chín, khô, dai hoặc không an toàn nếu xử lý kém. Tôi kiểm soát nhiệt, nấu đúng kết cấu và tránh nhiễm chéo.
Key words: overcooked, dry, rubbery, cross contamination
Assessor tip: Good answer shows both quality and safety.
Practice Card 4: How do you prepare vegetables for service?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
I wash, peel, trim and cut vegetables according to the prep list and recipe. I store them in clean containers, label them and keep them cold until service.
Tôi rửa, gọt, cắt tỉa và thái rau theo prep list và công thức. Tôi bảo quản trong hộp sạch, ghi nhãn và giữ lạnh đến khi phục vụ.
Key words: wash, peel, trim, prep list
Assessor tip: Use verbs in order.
Practice Card 5: How do you blanch vegetables?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
I cook vegetables briefly in boiling salted water, then refresh them in ice water to stop cooking and keep colour and texture.
Tôi chần rau nhanh trong nước muối sôi, rồi làm lạnh trong nước đá để dừng chín và giữ màu, kết cấu.
Key words: blanch, refresh, ice water, texture
Assessor tip: Blanching often appears in practical questions.
Practice Card 6: Why do you refresh vegetables after blanching?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
Refreshing stops the cooking process, keeps the vegetables crisp and helps maintain bright colour.
Làm lạnh sau khi chần giúp dừng quá trình chín, giữ rau giòn và màu đẹp.
Key words: refresh, crisp, bright colour
Assessor tip: Short answer is enough.
Practice Card 7: How do you cook vegetables for a buffet?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
I cook vegetables close to service time, keep them hot above 60°C or cold at 5°C or below, and replenish small amounts regularly.
Tôi nấu rau gần giờ phục vụ, giữ nóng trên 60°C hoặc giữ lạnh từ 5°C trở xuống, và bổ sung từng lượng nhỏ thường xuyên.
Key words: buffet, hot holding, replenish
Assessor tip: Connect to food safety and quality.
Practice Card 8: How do you choose vegetables for salad?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
I choose fresh, crisp vegetables with good colour, no slime, no mould and no unpleasant smell.
Tôi chọn rau tươi, giòn, màu đẹp, không nhớt, không mốc và không có mùi lạ.
Key words: fresh, crisp, slime, mould
Assessor tip: Quality indicators are important.
Practice Card 9: How do you make vinaigrette?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
A basic vinaigrette is usually three parts oil to one part vinegar, seasoned with salt and pepper. It can also include mustard, herbs or honey.
Vinaigrette cơ bản thường gồm 3 phần dầu và 1 phần giấm, nêm muối tiêu. Có thể thêm mù tạt, thảo mộc hoặc mật ong.
Key words: 3:1 ratio, oil, vinegar, mustard
Assessor tip: Remember the 3:1 ratio.
Practice Card 10: How do you keep salad fresh during service?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
I keep ingredients cold and dry, dress salads close to service and avoid mixing old salad with fresh salad.
Tôi giữ nguyên liệu lạnh và khô, trộn dressing gần lúc phục vụ và không trộn salad cũ với salad mới.
Key words: cold, dry, close to service
Assessor tip: Mention not mixing old and new food.
Practice Card 11: What is a composed salad?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
A composed salad is arranged carefully on the plate rather than simply tossed together. Ingredients are placed for colour, balance and presentation.
Composed salad là salad được sắp xếp cẩn thận trên đĩa thay vì chỉ trộn chung. Nguyên liệu được đặt để tạo màu sắc, cân bằng và trình bày đẹp.
Key words: arranged, colour, balance
Assessor tip: Useful for presentation questions.
Practice Card 12: How do you prevent soggy sandwiches?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
I use suitable bread, spread butter or mayonnaise as a barrier, drain wet fillings and assemble close to service.
Tôi dùng bánh phù hợp, phết bơ hoặc mayonnaise làm lớp chắn, để ráo nhân ướt và làm gần giờ phục vụ.
Key words: soggy, barrier, wet fillings
Assessor tip: Practical answer.
Practice Card 13: Why do you start stock with cold water?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
Cold water helps extract flavour and soluble proteins gradually from bones and vegetables, creating a better quality stock.
Nước lạnh giúp rút hương vị và protein hòa tan từ xương và rau củ từ từ, tạo stock chất lượng hơn.
Key words: cold water, extract flavour
Assessor tip: Common follow-up after stock question.
Practice Card 14: Why should stock be simmered, not boiled hard?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
Simmering keeps the stock clearer and allows impurities to rise so they can be skimmed. Boiling hard can make stock cloudy and greasy.
Hầm lăn tăn giúp stock trong hơn và tạp chất nổi lên để hớt. Sôi mạnh có thể làm stock đục và nhiều dầu.
Key words: simmer, skim, cloudy
Assessor tip: Very common SA question.
Practice Card 15: What do you do after making stock?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
I strain the stock, cool it quickly, remove excess fat, label it with date and store it in the fridge or freezer according to procedure.
Tôi lọc stock, làm nguội nhanh, loại bỏ mỡ thừa, ghi nhãn ngày và bảo quản trong tủ mát hoặc tủ đông theo quy trình.
Key words: strain, cool quickly, label
Assessor tip: Shows food safety.
Practice Card 16: How do you use stock in the kitchen?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
I use stock as a base for soups, sauces, stews, braises, risotto and some noodle dishes.
Tôi dùng stock làm nền cho súp, sốt, món hầm, món om, risotto và một số món mì.
Key words: base, soups, sauces, braises
Assessor tip: Give examples.
Practice Card 17: What is glace?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
Glace is a stock that has been reduced until it becomes very concentrated, rich and glossy.
Glace là stock được cô đặc đến khi đậm đặc, giàu vị và bóng.
Key words: reduced, concentrated, glossy
Assessor tip: Good to connect to demi-glace.
Practice Card 18: What is demi-glace?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
Demi-glace is a rich brown sauce made by reducing brown stock or espagnole-based sauce until it is thick and full of flavour.
Demi-glace là sốt nâu đậm được làm bằng cách cô đặc brown stock hoặc sốt nền espagnole đến khi đặc và đậm vị.
Key words: brown sauce, reduce, rich
Assessor tip: Do not overcomplicate.
Practice Card 19: What is Béchamel sauce?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
Béchamel is a white sauce made from milk and white roux. It is used in dishes such as lasagne, gratins and creamy sauces.
Béchamel là sốt trắng làm từ sữa và white roux. Dùng trong lasagne, gratin và các sốt kem.
Key words: milk, white roux, lasagne
Assessor tip: Remember base and uses.
Practice Card 20: What is Velouté sauce?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
Velouté is made from white stock and blond roux. It can be used as a base for chicken, fish or vegetable sauces.
Velouté làm từ white stock và blond roux. Nó dùng làm nền cho sốt gà, cá hoặc rau.
Key words: white stock, blond roux
Assessor tip: Compare with béchamel: milk vs stock.
Practice Card 21: What is Espagnole sauce?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
Espagnole is a brown mother sauce made with brown stock, brown roux and aromatics. It is a base for many brown sauces.
Espagnole là sốt mẹ màu nâu làm từ brown stock, brown roux và rau thơm. Nó là nền cho nhiều sốt nâu.
Key words: brown stock, brown roux
Assessor tip: Good for steak sauces.
Practice Card 22: What is tomato sauce in classical cookery?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
Tomato sauce is made from tomatoes, mirepoix, herbs and seasoning. It is cooked until the flavour develops and the texture is correct.
Tomato sauce làm từ cà chua, mirepoix, thảo mộc và gia vị. Nấu đến khi hương vị phát triển và kết cấu đúng.
Key words: tomato, mirepoix, herbs
Assessor tip: Mention texture and seasoning.
Practice Card 23: How do you fix a sauce that is too thick?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
I add a suitable liquid such as stock, milk, cream or water depending on the sauce, then whisk and adjust seasoning.
Tôi thêm chất lỏng phù hợp như stock, sữa, kem hoặc nước tùy loại sốt, rồi đánh đều và chỉnh gia vị.
Key words: thin down, suitable liquid, seasoning
Assessor tip: Shows control.
Practice Card 24: How do you fix a sauce that is too thin?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
I reduce it further or add an appropriate thickening agent such as roux, cornflour slurry or beurre manié, depending on the recipe.
Tôi cô đặc thêm hoặc thêm chất làm đặc phù hợp như roux, bột bắp pha nước hoặc beurre manié, tùy công thức.
Key words: reduce, slurry, thickening agent
Assessor tip: Good practical answer.
Practice Card 25: What is a cream soup?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
A cream soup is a thick soup usually made with stock, vegetables or main ingredients and finished with cream or milk.
Cream soup là súp đặc thường làm từ stock, rau hoặc nguyên liệu chính và hoàn thiện bằng kem hoặc sữa.
Key words: cream soup, finished with cream
Assessor tip: Give example mushroom soup.
Practice Card 26: What is a puree soup?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
A puree soup is made by cooking ingredients until soft and blending them until smooth, such as pumpkin soup or pea soup.
Puree soup làm bằng cách nấu nguyên liệu đến mềm rồi xay mịn, ví dụ pumpkin soup hoặc pea soup.
Key words: blend, smooth, pumpkin
Assessor tip: Simple definition.
Practice Card 27: What is a bisque?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
A bisque is a rich, smooth soup usually made from shellfish such as lobster, crab or prawns.
Bisque là súp mịn, đậm, thường làm từ shellfish như lobster, cua hoặc tôm.
Key words: shellfish, lobster, crab
Assessor tip: Mention seafood.
Practice Card 28: What is chowder?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
Chowder is a thick soup, often made with seafood or vegetables, milk or cream and sometimes potatoes.
Chowder là súp đặc, thường làm từ hải sản hoặc rau, sữa hoặc kem và đôi khi có khoai tây.
Key words: thick soup, seafood, cream
Assessor tip: Common soup type.
Practice Card 29: How do you cool soup safely?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
I divide soup into shallow containers, cool it quickly, label it and place it in the fridge once it is safe to do so according to our procedure.
Tôi chia súp vào khay nông, làm nguội nhanh, ghi nhãn và cho vào tủ mát khi an toàn theo quy trình.
Key words: shallow containers, cool quickly, label
Assessor tip: Connect to Book 2 food safety.
Practice Card 30: How do you garnish soup?
English model answer
Vietnamese translation
I choose a garnish that suits the soup, such as herbs, cream, croutons, oil, seafood or roasted seeds. The garnish must be edible and match the flavour.
Tôi chọn garnish phù hợp với súp như thảo mộc, kem, croutons, dầu, hải sản hoặc hạt rang. Garnish phải ăn được và hợp hương vị.
Key words: edible garnish, match flavour
Assessor tip: Presentation question.

Fast Revision Sheets
Topic
Must remember
Eggs
Low heat for scrambled eggs; gentle simmer for poached eggs; Hollandaise uses egg yolk and clarified butter.
Vegetables
Store clean, cold and separate from raw meat. Use correct cuts for recipe and presentation.
Salads
Four parts: base, body, garnish, dressing. Dress close to service.
Stocks
Start with cold water, simmer gently, skim, strain, cool quickly, label.
Sauces
Five mother sauces: Béchamel, Velouté, Espagnole, Tomato, Hollandaise.
Soups
Know clear, broth, puree, cream, bisque, chowder and cold soups.
Common Mistakes Vietnamese Candidates Make
Saying "boil stock" instead of "simmer stock gently".
Forgetting to mention storage, labelling and cooling after cooking.
Explaining a dish but not mentioning food safety.
Using long memorised answers that do not sound like real experience.
Confusing Béchamel and Velouté: Béchamel uses milk; Velouté uses stock.
Saying salad dressing is always added early. In most service situations, dress close to service to keep salad fresh.
Forgetting examples: always add one dish name if possible.

Explain three vegetable cuts.

Explain vinaigrette.

Explain how you make brown stock.
Explain why fish stock cooks briefly.
Explain what good stock looks like.
Explain the five mother sauces.
Explain what roux is.
Explain what emulsion is.
Explain Hollandaise sauce.
Explain how to fix a sauce that is too thick.
Explain soup types.
Explain pumpkin soup.
Explain bisque.
Explain how to cool soup safely.

1. How to Answer Menu Planning and Costing Questions
Core strategy
In a Skills Assessment interview, do not say only "This is the Head Chef's job."
A good cook can explain how they assist the chef: checking stock, portioning correctly, reducing waste, following SRC and understanding customer needs.
Use practical workplace examples. Keep answers clear, short and connected to food safety, quality and cost control.

Question type
What assessor wants to hear
Safe answer pattern
Menu planning
You understand season, customer needs, ingredient availability and kitchen capacity.
I assist my Head Chef by giving feedback, checking ingredients and suggesting practical options.
Food cost
You know waste, portion size and ingredient prices affect profit.
I follow SRC, control portions and report price changes.
Special diet
You take allergies and religious/health needs seriously.
I confirm the request, inform chef/FOH, check ingredients and avoid cross-contact.
Par stock
You understand stock levels and ordering.
I check stock, follow FIFO, monitor use-by dates and avoid over-ordering.
Waste control
You can reduce avoidable waste.
I store correctly, use trimmings appropriately and record wastage.
Vietnamese explanation
Khi bi hoi ve menu/costing, ban khong nen tra loi "khong phai viec cua toi" roi dung lai.
Hay noi: As a cook, I assist my Head Chef... Sau do dua vi du ve stock, portion, SRC, waste va dietary requirements.
Nguoi phong van muon biet ban co hieu van hanh bep, khong chi biet nau mon.

2. Menu Planning Fundamentals
Definition
Menu planning means selecting dishes that suit the restaurant concept, customer demand, season, ingredient availability, kitchen capacity, food cost and staff skills.
For a cook, the key role is to support the Head Chef by giving practical feedback and helping maintain consistency during production.

Menu factor
Meaning
Cook interview answer
Customer demand
What customers like and buy often.
I check popular dishes and customer feedback.
Seasonality
Ingredients available and suitable for the time of year.
I suggest seasonal ingredients because they are fresher and often cheaper.
Weather
Hot weather suits lighter dishes; cold weather suits soups/stews.
I consider what dishes are suitable for the season.
Kitchen capacity
Equipment, space and service speed.
I suggest dishes our kitchen can produce consistently during busy service.
Food cost
Ingredient cost compared with selling price.
I help by controlling portion size and reducing waste.
Skill level
Staff ability to produce the dish.
I follow the SRC and help train new staff when needed.
Dietary needs
Allergy, religion, health, lifestyle.
I check allergens and prepare special requests carefully.
Question 1: How do you help with menu planning?
Model Answer
Actually, final menu planning is mainly the Head Chef's responsibility. As a cook, I assist by giving feedback about ingredients, seasonality, customer preferences, preparation time and the practicality of producing the dish during busy service. I also follow the Standard Recipe Card to keep the dish consistent.

Dich tieng Viet
Thuc te, viec lap menu cuoi cung chu yeu la trach nhiem cua Head Chef. La mot cook, toi ho tro bang cach gop y ve nguyen lieu, mua vu, so thich cua khach, thoi gian chuan bi va tinh kha thi khi phuc vu gio cao diem. Toi cung lam theo SRC de mon an dong nhat.

Word
IPA
Meaning
Example
seasonality
/ˌsiːzəˈnæləti/
tinh mua vu
Seasonality affects menu planning.
practicality
/ˌpræktɪˈkæləti/
tinh thuc te
The dish must have practicality during service.
customer preference
/ˈkʌstəmə ˈprefrəns/
so thich khach hang
We consider customer preferences.
Common follow-up questions:
What menu items are popular in your restaurant?
How do you know if a dish is suitable for the menu?
Who makes the final menu decision?
Assessor Tip
Avoid saying "I do not know". Say clearly that you assist the Head Chef and explain your practical role.

Question 2: What do you consider when planning a menu?
Model Answer
I consider customer needs, food cost, ingredient availability, season, kitchen equipment, staff skills, service speed, special diets and food safety. A good menu should be practical, profitable, balanced and consistent.

Dich tieng Viet
Toi xem xet nhu cau khach hang, chi phi thuc pham, nguon nguyen lieu, mua vu, thiet bi bep, ky nang nhan vien, toc do phuc vu, che do an dac biet va an toan thuc pham. Mot menu tot can thuc te, co loi nhuan, can bang va dong nhat.

Word
IPA
Meaning
Example
profitable
/ˈprɒfɪtəbl/
co loi nhuan
The dish must be profitable.
balanced
/ˈbælənst/
can bang
A balanced menu has variety.
availability
/əˌveɪləˈbɪləti/
su san co
Ingredient availability is important.
Common follow-up questions:
Why is food cost important?
How do dietary requirements affect menu planning?
How does season affect the menu?
Assessor Tip
Use a list of 4-6 factors, not a long speech. Finish with a sentence about consistency and safety.

3. Standard Recipe Card (SRC)
SRC definition
A Standard Recipe Card is a document that gives all the information needed to make a dish consistently.
It normally includes dish name, ingredients, quantities, preparation steps, cooking method, equipment, portion size, garnish, plating photo/instructions, allergens, cost and yield.

SRC field
Purpose
Example
Dish name
Identify the dish
Grilled chicken with salad
Ingredients
Control quality and allergens
Chicken breast, lettuce, lemon
Quantity
Control portion and cost
250 g chicken per portion
Method
Keep cooking consistent
Grill to correct internal temperature
Portion size
Control cost and customer expectation
1 chicken breast + 80 g salad
Plating
Keep presentation consistent
Protein centre, salad left, sauce right
Allergens
Protect customers
Dairy, gluten, egg, nuts
Cost
Support pricing
Ingredient cost per portion
Question 3: What is a Standard Recipe Card?
Model Answer
A Standard Recipe Card is a document that shows the ingredients, quantities, preparation steps, cooking method, portion size, plating instructions and sometimes food cost and allergen information. It helps every cook produce the same dish with the same quality.

Dich tieng Viet
Standard Recipe Card la tai lieu ghi nguyen lieu, dinh luong, cac buoc chuan bi, phuong phap nau, khau phan, cach trinh bay va doi khi co food cost/allergen. No giup moi cook lam mon an dong nhat ve chat luong.

Word
IPA
Meaning
Example
quantity
/ˈkwɒntəti/
so luong/dinh luong
Check the quantity.
plating instructions
/ˈpleɪtɪŋ ɪnˈstrʌkʃnz/
huong dan trinh bay
Follow plating instructions.
allergen information
/ˈælədʒən ˌɪnfəˈmeɪʃn/
thong tin di ung
Check allergen information.
Common follow-up questions:
Why do you follow SRC?
What information is included in a recipe card?
How does SRC help food cost?
Assessor Tip
When asked about SRC, always mention consistency, portion control, quality, training and food cost.

Question 4: Why do you need to follow the Standard Recipe Card?
Model Answer
I follow the SRC to make sure the dish is consistent every time. It helps control portion size, food cost, quality, presentation, allergens and waste. It also makes it easier to train new staff.

Dich tieng Viet
Toi lam theo SRC de dam bao mon an luc nao cung dong nhat. SRC giup kiem soat khau phan, chi phi, chat luong, trinh bay, di ung va lang phi. No cung giup dao tao nhan vien moi de hon.

Word
IPA
Meaning
Example
portion size
/ˈpɔːʃn saɪz/
kich co khau phan
SRC controls portion size.
waste
/weɪst/
lang phi
It reduces waste.
train staff
/treɪn stɑːf/
dao tao nhan vien
It helps train staff.
Common follow-up questions:
What happens if cooks do not follow SRC?
Who prepares the SRC in your kitchen?
Can you change the recipe yourself?
Assessor Tip
Say you do not change recipes without approval. This shows discipline and teamwork.

4. Portion Control
Definition
Portion control means serving the correct amount of food for each dish according to the SRC.
It protects food cost and gives customers consistent value.

Course/item
Example portion
Notes
Starter
80-120 g
Depends on dish type
Soup
200-250 ml
Use ladle/measure cup
Main protein
180-250 g
Depends on menu and SRC
Vegetable garnish
50-80 g
Balance colour and nutrition
Dessert
80-120 g
Use mould/scoop/scale
Sauce
30-60 ml
Control with ladle/squeeze bottle
Question 5: What is portion control and why is it important?
Model Answer
Portion control means measuring and serving the correct amount of food for each dish. It is important because it keeps the dish consistent, controls food cost, reduces waste and ensures customers receive the correct portion every time.

Dich tieng Viet
Portion control nghia la do luong va phuc vu dung luong thuc an cho tung mon. No quan trong vi giu mon an dong nhat, kiem soat chi phi, giam lang phi va dam bao khach nhan dung khau phan.

Word
IPA
Meaning
Example
measure
/ˈmeʒə/
do luong
Measure the portion.
scale
/skeɪl/
can
Use a scale.
consistent value
/kənˈsɪstənt ˈvæljuː/
gia tri dong nhat
Customers receive consistent value.
Common follow-up questions:
What tools do you use for portion control?
How does portion control affect profit?
What happens if portions are too big?
Assessor Tip
Mention tools: scales, cups, spoons, ladles, scoops and SRC.

Question 6: How do you control portion size in your kitchen?
Model Answer
I use scales, measuring cups, ladles, scoops and the Standard Recipe Card. I also follow the Head Chef's instructions and check plating photos to make sure the portion and presentation are consistent.

Dich tieng Viet
Toi dung can, coc do, muoi, muong dinh luong va SRC. Toi cung lam theo chi dan cua Head Chef va kiem tra hinh trinh bay de dam bao khau phan va cach trinh bay dong nhat.

Word
IPA
Meaning
Example
ladle
/ˈleɪdl/
muoi canh
Use a ladle for soup.
scoop
/skuːp/
muong xuc
Use a scoop for dessert.
plating photo
/ˈpleɪtɪŋ ˈfəʊtəʊ/
hinh trinh bay
Check the plating photo.
Common follow-up questions:
What if a customer asks for a bigger portion?
What if a cook serves too much food?
Who checks portion size?
Assessor Tip
Use one real tool example. For example: "For soup, I use a 250 ml ladle."

5. Food Cost, Selling Price and Labour Cost
Key idea
A cook does not always decide the final selling price, but a cook must understand that ingredient prices, waste, portion size and labour affect the restaurant profit.
In an interview, show that you know how your daily work affects cost.

Term
Meaning
Simple formula/example
Food cost per portion
Cost of ingredients for one serve
Chicken 3.20 + salad 1.10 + sauce 0.50 = 4.80
Food cost percentage
Food cost compared with selling price
Food cost / selling price x 100
Gross profit
Selling price minus food cost
24.00 - 4.80 = 19.20
Labour cost
Cost of staff time
Preparation and service time affects cost
Yield
Usable product after trimming/cooking
More trim = lower yield = higher cost
Question 7: How do you help control food cost?
Model Answer
As a cook, I help control food cost by following the Standard Recipe Card, controlling portion sizes, reducing waste, using FIFO, storing food correctly and informing the Head Chef if ingredient prices or quality change.

Dich tieng Viet
La mot cook, toi giup kiem soat food cost bang cach lam theo SRC, kiem soat khau phan, giam lang phi, dung FIFO, bao quan dung va bao cho Head Chef neu gia nguyen lieu hoac chat luong thay doi.

Word
IPA
Meaning
Example
ingredient price
/ɪnˈɡriːdiənt praɪs/
gia nguyen lieu
Ingredient prices can change.
reduce waste
/rɪˈdjuːs weɪst/
giam lang phi
Reduce waste every shift.
cost control
/kɒst kənˈtrəʊl/
kiem soat chi phi
Cost control is important.
Common follow-up questions:
How does FIFO help food cost?
What do you do if ingredient price increases?
How do portions affect food cost?
Assessor Tip
Do not overcomplicate formulas unless asked. Explain what you do practically in the kitchen.

Question 8: What would you do if food cost becomes too high?
Model Answer
I would report it to the Head Chef. We can check portion sizes, reduce waste, review suppliers, use seasonal ingredients, adjust the recipe if approved, or consider a suitable substitute without reducing dish quality.

Dich tieng Viet
Toi se bao cho Head Chef. Chung toi co the kiem tra khau phan, giam lang phi, xem lai nha cung cap, dung nguyen lieu theo mua, dieu chinh cong thuc neu duoc phe duyet, hoac thay the nguyen lieu phu hop ma khong giam chat luong mon.

Word
IPA
Meaning
Example
substitute
/ˈsʌbstɪtjuːt/
thay the
Use a suitable substitute.
supplier
/səˈplaɪə/
nha cung cap
Review suppliers.
approved
/əˈpruːvd/
duoc phe duyet
Only change recipes if approved.
Common follow-up questions:
Can you change ingredients yourself?
How do you keep quality if you substitute ingredients?
Why are seasonal ingredients useful?
Assessor Tip
Always say "with approval" when changing recipes or ingredients.

Question 9: What is labour cost?
Model Answer
Labour cost is the cost of staff time needed to prepare and serve food. A dish that takes too much preparation time can increase labour cost, so we need good mise en place, simple workflow and efficient service.

Dich tieng Viet
Labour cost la chi phi thoi gian nhan vien de chuan bi va phuc vu mon. Mon mat qua nhieu thoi gian co the lam tang chi phi nhan cong, vi vay can mise en place tot, quy trinh don gian va phuc vu hieu qua.

Word
IPA
Meaning
Example
staff time
/stɑːf taɪm/
thoi gian nhan vien
Staff time affects labour cost.
workflow
/ˈwɜːkfləʊ/
quy trinh lam viec
Improve workflow.
efficient
/ɪˈfɪʃnt/
hieu qua
Efficient service controls cost.
Common follow-up questions:
How do you reduce labour cost without reducing quality?
Why is mise en place important for labour cost?
How does menu complexity affect labour cost?
Assessor Tip
Connect labour cost with mise en place, time management and simple production.

6. Par Stock, Ordering and Inventory Control
Definition
Par stock is the minimum and maximum stock level needed to run the business smoothly without overstocking.
Good par stock control prevents shortage, over-ordering, waste and storage problems.

Stock issue
Risk
Correct action
Too much stock
Waste, poor quality, no storage space
Reduce order, use FIFO, monitor use-by dates
Too little stock
Menu items unavailable, poor service
Check bookings and order in advance
Wrong stock level
Cost and service problems
Stocktake, record and report
Near expiry
Waste risk
Use first, specials/promotion if approved
Question 10: What is par stock level?
Model Answer
Par stock level is the minimum and maximum amount of stock needed to run the kitchen smoothly. It helps avoid running out of ingredients and also prevents over-ordering and waste.

Dich tieng Viet
Par stock la muc ton kho toi thieu va toi da can thiet de bep van hanh tron tru. No giup tranh thieu nguyen lieu va tranh dat qua nhieu gay lang phi.

Word
IPA
Meaning
Example
minimum
/ˈmɪnɪməm/
toi thieu
Minimum stock level
maximum
/ˈmæksɪməm/
toi da
Maximum stock level
over-ordering
/ˌəʊvər ˈɔːdərɪŋ/
dat qua nhieu
Over-ordering causes waste.
Common follow-up questions:
What happens if there is too much stock?
How do you check stock?
How do bookings affect par stock?
Assessor Tip
Mention two levels: minimum and maximum.

Question 11: How do you know how much food to prepare?
Model Answer
I check bookings, previous sales, the prep list, par stock level, customer orders and Standard Recipe Cards. During service, I communicate with my team and adjust preparation if needed.

Dich tieng Viet
Toi kiem tra dat ban, doanh so cu, prep list, par stock, order cua khach va SRC. Trong gio phuc vu, toi giao tiep voi doi bep va dieu chinh neu can.

dieu chinh

Common follow-up questions:
Who prepares the prep list?
What if tomorrow is a busy day?
How do you avoid overproduction?
Assessor Tip
This is a very common question. Answer with bookings + prep list + SRC + par stock.

7. Yield Test, Waste Control and Sustainability
Yield test
A yield test calculates how much usable product remains after trimming, peeling, cooking or portioning.
Yield affects food cost because expensive waste increases the real cost of each portion.

Waste control method
Example
Interview phrase
FIFO
Use older stock first
I rotate stock to reduce expired items.
Correct storage
Seal, label, date
Correct storage maintains quality.
Use edible trimmings
Stocks, sauces, soups, fillings
I use suitable trimmings instead of wasting them.
Portion control
Scale protein portions
Correct portions prevent over-serving.
Record waste
Waste sheet
Recording waste helps identify problems.
Batch preparation
Prepare as needed
I avoid overproduction.
Question 12: How do you control wastage?
Model Answer
I control wastage by following FIFO, checking use-by dates, labelling food, storing items correctly, preparing the correct quantity, using suitable trimmings for stocks or sauces and recording waste when required.

Dich tieng Viet
Toi kiem soat lang phi bang FIFO, kiem tra han su dung, ghi nhan, bao quan dung, chuan bi dung so luong, tan dung phan cat phu hop de lam stock/sauce va ghi chep waste khi can.

Word
IPA
Meaning
Example
wastage
/ˈweɪstɪdʒ/
su lang phi
Control wastage.
trimmings
/ˈtrɪmɪŋz/
phan cat bo con dung duoc
Use trimmings for stock.
record waste
/rɪˈkɔːd weɪst/
ghi chep lang phi
Record waste when required.
Common follow-up questions:
Can you give an example of using trimmings?
How does FIFO reduce waste?
What do you do with expired food?
Assessor Tip
Give practical examples: vegetable trimmings for stock; beef bones for brown stock; bread offcuts for croutons.

Question 13: How do you work sustainably in the kitchen?
Model Answer
I reduce, reuse and recycle where possible. I avoid overproduction, store food correctly, turn off equipment when not in use, use water carefully and separate recyclable waste according to the restaurant procedure.

Dich tieng Viet
Toi giam, tai su dung va tai che neu co the. Toi tranh san xuat qua nhieu, bao quan dung, tat thiet bi khi khong dung, tiet kiem nuoc va phan loai rac tai che theo quy trinh nha hang.

Word
IPA
Meaning
Example
sustainable
/səˈsteɪnəbl/

overproduction
/ˌəʊvəprəˈdʌkʃn/

recyclable
/ˌriːˈsaɪkləbl/

Common follow-up questions:
How do you reduce energy use?
How do you reduce food waste?
What can be recycled in a kitchen?
Assessor Tip
Keep sustainability answers simple: reduce waste, save energy, save water, recycle.

8. Buffet Planning and Quantity Calculations
Buffet planning rule
Buffet planning requires correct quantity, food safety, temperature control, attractive presentation and regular replenishment.
Modern buffets often use smaller platters and replenish more often to maintain quality and reduce waste.

Buffet concern
What to do
Why
Hot food
Hold above 60 C
Keep food out of the danger zone
Cold food
Keep at 5 C or below where possible
Reduce bacterial growth
Replenishment
Use smaller trays and refill often
Maintain freshness and reduce waste
Cross-contact
Separate utensils and labels
Protect customers with allergies
Portion estimates
Use guest number + extra buffer
Avoid shortage and overproduction
Question 14: How do you plan food quantity for a buffet?
Model Answer
I check the number of guests, type of function, time of service, menu style, portion size, special dietary requirements and a reasonable extra buffer. I also plan how to hold hot food and cold food safely during service.

Dich tieng Viet
Toi kiem tra so khach, loai su kien, thoi gian phuc vu, kieu menu, khau phan, yeu cau an uong dac biet va mot phan du hop ly. Toi cung len ke hoach giu nong/giu lanh an toan.

Word
IPA
Meaning
Example
buffer
/ˈbʌfə/
phan du phong
Prepare a small buffer.
function
/ˈfʌŋkʃn/
su kien/tiec
Type of function
replenish
/rɪˈplenɪʃ/
bo sung lai
Replenish the buffet regularly.
Common follow-up questions:
How much salad for 120 guests at 50 g each?
How do you keep hot food safe?
How do you reduce buffet waste?
Assessor Tip
If a calculation appears, say the formula aloud: guests x grams per guest + extra.

Calculation question
Working
Answer
50 g salad per person for 120 guests + 1 kg extra
120 x 50 g = 6000 g = 6 kg; + 1 kg
7 kg salad
75 g ham per person for 120 guests + 2 kg extra
120 x 75 g = 9000 g = 9 kg; + 2 kg
11 kg ham
Soup 250 ml per guest for 80 guests
80 x 250 ml = 20,000 ml
20 litres soup
Dessert 100 g per guest for 60 guests + 10% extra
60 x 100 g = 6 kg; + 0.6 kg
6.6 kg dessert
9. Special Dietary Requirements
Interview principle
Never guess with special dietary requests. Confirm the requirement, check the recipe, check labels, inform the Head Chef/FOH and prevent cross-contact.
For allergies, a small mistake can be serious. Use clean equipment, clean surfaces and separate utensils.

Diet
Avoid / requirement
Safe cook response
Vegetarian
No meat or fish; some may eat dairy/eggs
Use vegetable stock, separate utensils and no meat garnish.
Vegan
No animal products
No meat, fish, dairy, eggs, honey; use plant-based ingredients.
Coeliac / gluten-free
No gluten from wheat/barley/rye
Check labels; avoid flour, breadcrumbs and cross-contact.
Lactose-free
Avoid lactose in milk/dairy
Use lactose-free or plant-based alternatives.
Diabetic
Control sugar/refined carbohydrates
Avoid high-sugar items; balance protein, vegetables and suitable carbs.
Low-calorie
Lower energy intake
Use grilling, steaming or poaching; avoid deep-frying/cream-heavy sauces.
Heart-friendly
Lower saturated fat and salt
Use lean proteins, vegetables and lighter sauces.
Halal / Muslim
No pork; halal meat; no alcohol
Check supplier/ingredients; avoid alcohol-based sauces.
Kosher
Specific Jewish dietary rules
Follow certified requirements; do not mix meat and dairy.
Hindu
Often no beef; varies by guest
Confirm with guest/FOH; avoid beef if required.
Question 15: What are special dietary requirements?
Model Answer
Special dietary requirements are customer needs related to religion, health, allergy, intolerance or personal lifestyle. Examples include vegetarian, vegan, halal, kosher, gluten-free, lactose-free and diabetic diets.

Dich tieng Viet
Special dietary requirements la cac yeu cau an uong lien quan den ton giao, suc khoe, di ung, khong dung nap hoac loi song ca nhan. Vi du: vegetarian, vegan, halal, kosher, gluten-free, lactose-free va diabetic diets.

Word
IPA
Meaning
Example
dietary requirement
/ˈdaɪətəri rɪˈkwaɪəmənt/
yeu cau an uong
Confirm dietary requirements.
cross-contact
/krɒs ˈkɒntækt/
tiep xuc cheo
Avoid cross-contact.
intolerance
/ɪnˈtɒlərəns/
khong dung nap
Lactose intolerance.
Common follow-up questions:
How do you communicate this with FOH?
What if you are not sure?
How do you prevent cross-contact?
Assessor Tip
For special diets, safety is more important than speed. Always confirm, check and communicate.

Question 16: What do you do if a customer has a food allergy?
Model Answer
I take it seriously. I inform the Head Chef and FOH, check the ingredients and labels, avoid cross-contact, use clean equipment and prepare the dish according to the confirmed requirement. If I am not sure, I do not guess.

Dich tieng Viet
Toi xu ly nghiem tuc. Toi bao Head Chef va FOH, kiem tra nguyen lieu/nhan, tranh nhiem cheo, dung thiet bi sach va lam mon theo yeu cau da xac nhan. Neu khong chac, toi khong doan.

Question 17: What is a vegan diet?
Model Answer
A vegan diet avoids all animal products, including meat, fish, seafood, eggs, dairy, butter, cream and honey. I would use plant-based ingredients and check sauces, stocks and dressings carefully.

Question 18: What is a coeliac or gluten-free diet?
Model Answer
A coeliac or gluten-free diet avoids gluten, which is found in wheat, barley and rye. I avoid flour, breadcrumbs, pasta and many sauces unless they are confirmed gluten-free. I also prevent cross-contact.

Dich tieng Viet
Coeliac/gluten-free la che do khong dung gluten trong lua mi, lua mach va lua mach den. Toi tranh flour, breadcrumbs, pasta va nhieu loai sauce tru khi xac nhan gluten-free. Toi cung tranh cross-contact.

Question 19: What would you do for a diabetic customer?
Model Answer
I would avoid high-sugar items, use balanced portions, choose grilled, steamed or poached items where suitable and confirm the request with FOH or the Head Chef. I would not make medical advice; I follow the restaurant procedure.

Dich tieng Viet
Voi khach diabetic, toi tranh mon nhieu duong, dung khau phan can bang, chon mon grilled/steamed/poached neu phu hop va xac nhan voi FOH/Head Chef. Toi khong tu dua loi khuyen y te; toi lam theo quy trinh nha hang.

Question 20: What would you do if a customer is allergic to seafood?
Model Answer
I would remove seafood from the dish and suggest another protein such as chicken, beef, pork or vegetables if suitable. I would use clean equipment, separate utensils and avoid cooking near seafood to prevent cross-contact.

Dich tieng Viet
Neu khach di ung hai san, toi loai bo hai san va de xuat protein khac nhu ga, bo, heo hoac rau neu phu hop. Toi dung thiet bi sach, dung cu rieng va tranh nhiem cheo voi hai san.

10. Allergens and Special Requests Procedure
Step
Action
Why
1. Receive request
Listen and repeat the request to confirm
Avoid misunderstanding
2. Inform chef/FOH
Tell the Head Chef and FOH clearly
Team awareness
3. Check ingredients
Check recipe, labels, sauces, stocks, garnishes
Hidden allergens may exist
4. Prepare safely
Clean bench, clean tools, separate utensils
Avoid cross-contact
5. Serve correctly
Mark ticket/plate and communicate to FOH
Correct dish to correct guest
6. If unsure
Stop and ask; do not guess
Protect customer safety
Common allergens to remember
Peanuts, tree nuts, milk/dairy, eggs, fish, shellfish, soy, sesame, wheat/gluten and sulphites are common examples assessors may ask about.
Your answer should focus on checking labels, avoiding cross-contact and communicating clearly.

Question 21: How do you handle a nut allergy order?
Model Answer
I confirm the allergy with FOH, inform the Head Chef, check all ingredients and labels, remove nuts from the dish, use clean equipment and avoid cross-contact. If I cannot guarantee safety, I tell the Head Chef instead of guessing.

Dich tieng Viet
Toi xac nhan voi FOH, bao Head Chef, kiem tra nguyen lieu/nhan, bo hat khoi mon, dung thiet bi sach va tranh cross-contact. Neu khong dam bao an toan, toi bao Head Chef thay vi doan.

Word
IPA
Meaning
Example
guarantee
/ˌɡærənˈtiː/
dam bao
Do not guarantee if unsure.
label
/ˈleɪbl/
nhan
Check the label.
utensil
/juːˈtensl/
dung cu
Use separate utensils.
Common follow-up questions:
Can you just remove the nuts?
What if the sauce may contain nuts?
Who should be informed?
Assessor Tip
Do not say "I just remove the nuts". Cross-contact can still be dangerous.

11. Nutrition Basics and Five Food Groups
Interview use
You do not need to be a dietitian, but a cook should understand basic nutrition, balanced meals and special diet requests.
Use simple, safe answers. Do not give medical advice.

Food group
Examples
Main nutrients / role
Vegetables and legumes
Broccoli, carrots, beans
Fibre, vitamins, minerals
Fruit
Apple, banana, berries
Vitamins, fibre, natural sugars
Grains/cereals
Rice, pasta, bread, oats
Carbohydrates, energy, fibre
Lean meats and alternatives
Chicken, beef, fish, tofu, eggs
Protein, iron, B vitamins
Dairy and alternatives
Milk, yoghurt, cheese, soy milk
Calcium, protein
Question 22: What are the five food groups?
Model Answer
The five food groups are vegetables and legumes, fruit, grains or cereals, lean meat and alternatives, and dairy or dairy alternatives. A balanced menu should include variety from these groups where suitable.

Dich tieng Viet
Nam nhom thuc pham gom rau va dau, trai cay, ngu coc, thit nac va cac nguon thay the, sua va san pham thay the. Menu can bang nen co su da dang tu cac nhom nay neu phu hop.

/ˈleɡjuːmz/

lua chon thay the

Common follow-up questions:
What is protein used for?
What foods are high in calcium?
What foods are high in Vitamin C?
Assessor Tip
Keep it basic. The assessor is checking kitchen knowledge, not medical expertise.

12. Australian Kitchen English for Menu and Costing
Expression
Meaning
Natural sentence
86 an item
Remove item from menu temporarily
We had to 86 the barramundi because stock was low.
Special
Temporary dish or promotion
We used near-expiry vegetables in a soup special.
GP / gross profit
Profit before other overheads
The Head Chef checks GP when pricing dishes.
Prep level
Amount prepared before service
Keep the prep level suitable for bookings.
Food cost
Ingredient cost
Portion control helps food cost.
Yield
Usable amount after trimming/cooking

Replacing an ingredient
Substitution must not reduce quality.

13. Interview Question Bank
No.
Question
Short answer
1
How do you plan a menu?
I consider customer needs, season, ingredient availability, food cost, kitchen equipment, staff skills, service speed and dietary requirements.
2
What is food cost?
Food cost is the ingredient cost of producing a dish or portion.
3
How do you control portion?
I follow SRC and use scales, cups, scoops and ladles.
4
What happens if portions are too large?
Food cost increases, profit decreases and customers receive inconsistent dishes.
5
What happens if portions are too small?
Customers may complain and the restaurant loses consistency and value.
6
How do you reduce waste?
I follow FIFO, label food, store correctly, prepare correct quantity and use suitable trimmings.
7
What is par stock?
It is the minimum and maximum stock needed for the kitchen to run smoothly.
8
What happens if there is too much stock?
Waste increases, quality drops and storage space becomes limited.
9
What happens if there is not enough stock?
Menu items may be unavailable and service quality may suffer.
10
What is yield?
Yield is the usable amount of product after trimming, peeling or cooking.
11
How does yield affect cost?
Low yield increases the real cost per portion.
12
What is a special diet?
It is a customer requirement related to health, allergy, religion or lifestyle.
13
What is vegetarian?
A vegetarian diet avoids meat and fish, but some vegetarians may eat eggs or dairy.
14
What is vegan?
A vegan diet avoids all animal products including dairy, eggs and honey.
15
What is gluten-free?
It avoids gluten from wheat, barley and rye.
16
What is lactose intolerance?
It means the person cannot tolerate lactose in milk and dairy products.
17
What should Muslims avoid?
Generally pork and alcohol; halal meat may be required.
18
What should many Hindu customers avoid?
Many avoid beef, but requirements should be confirmed.
19
How do you handle allergy requests?
I confirm the request, inform chef/FOH, check ingredients, use clean equipment and avoid cross-contact.
20
What if you are not sure about an ingredient?
I stop and ask the Head Chef. I do not guess.
21
Why is menu balance important?
It gives customers variety and supports nutrition, cost and kitchen workflow.
22
How do you price a dish?
Usually the Head Chef or manager prices it, but food cost, labour, portion and market price are considered.
23
What is labour cost?
It is the cost of staff time needed to prepare and serve food.
24
How do you reduce labour cost?
Good mise en place, simple workflow and efficient preparation help reduce labour cost.
25
What is a profitable dish?
A dish that customers like and that can be produced consistently with good food cost and quality.
26
How do seasonal ingredients help?
They are often fresher, better quality and sometimes cheaper.
27
What is a substitute ingredient?
It is an alternative ingredient used when the original is unavailable or too expensive.
28
Can you change a recipe yourself?
No, I need approval from the Head Chef before changing a recipe.
29
How do you handle a diabetic request?
I avoid high-sugar items and confirm the suitable option with the Head Chef/FOH.
30
How do you handle a coeliac request?
I avoid gluten, check labels and prevent cross-contact.
31
How do you calculate buffet quantity?
Guest number multiplied by portion size, plus a reasonable extra buffer.
32
How do you minimise buffet waste?
Use smaller platters, replenish regularly and prepare quantities based on guest numbers.
33
What is the role of SRC in menu planning?
SRC supports consistency, training, costing and portion control.
34
What is the role of FOH in special diets?
FOH confirms customer requirements and communicates them clearly to the kitchen.
35
How do you communicate special requests?
I repeat the request, confirm it and make sure the team understands it.
14. Scenario Practice Cards
Scenario 1
Situation
A customer asks for gluten-free pasta but the kitchen is very busy.

Best response
Confirm the request, check gluten-free pasta availability, use clean pot/water/utensils if procedure requires, inform FOH of timing and avoid cross-contact.

Scenario 2
Situation
The cost of salmon increases suddenly.

Best response
Report to Head Chef, check supplier options, review portion size and possible substitute, but do not change recipe without approval.

Scenario 3
Situation
You have too much chicken close to use-by date.

Best response
Check quality, inform Head Chef, use FIFO, suggest a special if approved, and avoid using any unsafe or expired product.

Scenario 4
Situation
A vegan guest orders a salad with Caesar dressing.

Best response
Explain through FOH that Caesar dressing may contain egg/anchovy/dairy, then offer vegan dressing if available.

Scenario 5
Situation
A customer is allergic to seafood but orders a dish cooked near prawns.

Best response
Inform Head Chef, use clean equipment and separate area if possible; if safety cannot be guaranteed, communicate honestly through FOH.

Scenario 6
Situation
The buffet has too much food left over.

Best response
Record waste, review quantities, use smaller platters next time and adjust production based on real guest numbers.

15. Mini Tests
Mini Test 1 - Costing and portion
1. What is portion control?
2. Name three tools for portion control.
3. If a dish costs $6 to produce and sells for $24, what is the food cost percentage?
4. What happens if portions are too large?
5. Why does low yield increase food cost?

Mini Test 2 - Special diets
1. What is vegan?
2. What should a coeliac customer avoid?
3. What should you do if a customer has a nut allergy?
4. Why should you not guess with special requests?
5. Give two examples of religious dietary requirements.

Answer Key
1. Portion control = serving correct amount according to SRC.
2. Scales, measuring cups, ladles, scoops, spoons.
3. 6 / 24 x 100 = 25%.
4. Food cost increases and consistency decreases.
5. More trimming/waste means less usable product from the same purchase cost.
Special diets: vegan avoids animal products; coeliac avoids gluten; allergy orders require confirmation, label checking and cross-contact control.

16. 14-Day Study Plan
Day
Task
Day 1
Read menu planning factors. Speak Q1-Q2.
Day 2
Study SRC. Practise SRC definition and benefits.
Day 3
Study portion control and tools. Record your answer.
Day 4
Study food cost and selling price basics.
Day 5
Study labour cost and workflow.
Day 6
Study par stock and ordering. Practise examples.
Day 7
Review yield, waste and sustainability.
Day 8
Study buffet planning calculations.
Day 9
Study vegetarian, vegan, halal, kosher, Hindu.
Day 10
Study gluten-free, lactose-free, diabetic and low-calorie.
Day 11
Study allergen procedure and cross-contact.
Day 12
Practise the 35-question bank aloud.
Day 13
Do scenario cards without notes.
Day 14
Mock interview: 20 random questions, 45-60 seconds each.
Final Checklist Before Interview
I can explain what a Standard Recipe Card is.
I can explain why portion control affects food cost.
I can answer how I help the Head Chef plan a menu.
I can explain par stock, overstocking and understocking.
I can calculate simple buffet quantities.
I can explain vegetarian, vegan, gluten-free, lactose-free and diabetic diets.
I can handle allergy requests safely and never guess.
I can speak each answer for 45-60 seconds with one workplace example.

18. Answer Templates for Speaking
Situation
Ready-to-use answer structure
Menu planning answer template
Final menu planning is mainly the Head Chef's responsibility. As a cook, I assist by [checking stock / suggesting seasonal ingredients / giving feedback]. I also consider [food cost / preparation time / customer needs] and follow the SRC to maintain consistency.
Costing answer template
As a cook, I help control food cost by following the SRC, using correct portions, reducing waste, checking labels and using FIFO. If ingredient prices change, I inform the Head Chef.
Special diet answer template
First, I confirm the request with FOH or the Head Chef. Then I check the recipe, labels and ingredients. I use clean equipment and avoid cross-contact. If I am not sure, I ask instead of guessing.
Buffet planning answer template
I calculate the quantity based on the number of guests, portion size and a reasonable extra buffer. I also plan hot holding, cold holding, replenishment and special dietary items.
Waste control answer template
I reduce waste by preparing the correct quantity, following FIFO, storing food correctly, using suitable trimmings and recording waste when required.
19. Common Mistakes Vietnamese Candidates Make
Common mistake
Correct approach
Saying: Menu planning is not my job.
Better: Final menu planning is the Head Chef's job, but I assist by giving feedback and controlling production.
Forgetting to mention approval before changing ingredients.
Better: I can suggest substitutes, but I only change the recipe with approval.
Only saying "I use FIFO" when asked about waste.
Better: Add labelling, storage, portion control, edible trimmings and waste records.
Saying "I remove nuts" for nut allergy.
Better: Mention labels, clean equipment and cross-contact.
Giving medical advice for diabetic diets.
Better: Say you follow restaurant procedure and confirmed requirements; do not provide medical advice.
Confusing vegan and vegetarian.
Better: Vegan avoids all animal products; vegetarian avoids meat/fish but may allow eggs/dairy depending on the customer.
Giving too long answers.
Better: 4-6 clear points plus one example.
Not using kitchen vocabulary.
Better: Use SRC, par stock, portion control, food cost, yield, cross-contact.
Not giving examples.
Better: Give one example from your kitchen, such as soup portions, salad portions or buffet quantity.
Ignoring FOH communication.
Better: Say you confirm special requests with FOH and Head Chef.
20. Extended Interview Questions (36-70)
No.
Question
Model short answer
36
What is menu balance?
Menu balance means offering variety in flavour, texture, colour, cooking methods, price and dietary options.
37
Why is seasonality important?
Seasonal ingredients are usually fresher, more available and sometimes cheaper.
38
How do customer needs affect menu planning?
A menu should match what customers want, including popular dishes and special dietary needs.
39
How do you choose a substitute ingredient?
I choose a suitable ingredient with similar function and quality, and I get approval from the Head Chef.
40
What is overproduction?
Overproduction means preparing more food than needed, which increases waste and cost.
41
What is underproduction?
Underproduction means not preparing enough food, which can delay service and disappoint customers.
42
How do you use sales records?
Sales records help the chef understand which dishes are popular and how much stock to prepare.
43
What is a special?
A special is a temporary dish, often using seasonal ingredients or available stock.
44
How do specials help waste control?
Specials can use safe ingredients that are close to use-by date or slow-moving stock.
45
What is gross profit?
Gross profit is selling price minus food cost.
46
What is food cost percentage?
It is food cost divided by selling price, multiplied by 100.
47
What is a costed recipe?
A costed recipe shows the ingredient cost for each portion.
48
How do you control sauce portions?
I use a ladle, spoon, squeeze bottle or measured container according to the SRC.
49
How do you control soup portions?
I use the correct ladle size, usually around 200-250 ml depending on the SRC.
50
How do you control steak portions?
I weigh portions using scales and follow the recipe card.
51
What is a yield test?
A yield test calculates how much usable product remains after trimming or cooking.
52
Give an example of edible trimmings.
Vegetable trimmings can be used for stock; bread offcuts can become croutons.
53
What is a requisition?
A requisition is a request for stock or ingredients from storage or purchasing.
54
How do you check stock before ordering?
I check the cool room, freezer, dry store, use-by dates and par levels.
55
How do dietary requirements affect menu planning?
The menu should include safe options or adaptable dishes for common dietary needs.
56
What is an allergen matrix?
It is a list showing which menu items contain common allergens.
57
What is cross-contact?
It is when an allergen accidentally touches a food that should not contain it.
58
How do you avoid gluten cross-contact?
Use clean equipment, separate utensils and avoid shared flour, crumbs or contaminated surfaces.
59
What is plant-based food?
Food made from plants rather than animal products.
60
What is halal?
Halal refers to food permitted under Islamic dietary rules.
61
What is kosher?
Kosher refers to food prepared according to Jewish dietary rules.
62
Why should menus show allergens?
It helps customers and staff identify risks and handle special requests safely.
63
How do you handle a customer who asks for no dairy?
Check milk, cream, butter, cheese and sauces; use dairy-free alternatives if available.
64
How do you make a dish lower calorie?
Use lighter cooking methods such as grilling, steaming or poaching, and avoid heavy cream or deep frying.
65
How can you make a dessert suitable for a diabetic customer?
Reduce sugar, use smaller portions and follow approved recipes or customer requirements.
66
What is menu engineering?
It is analysing dish popularity and profitability to improve the menu.
67
What is a high-profit dish?
A dish with good selling price, controlled food cost and strong customer demand.
68
Why should a menu not be too large?
A very large menu can increase stock, waste, labour and inconsistency.
69
How does equipment affect menu planning?
The kitchen must have suitable equipment and capacity to produce the menu consistently.
70
How does staff skill affect menu planning?
The team must be able to produce dishes safely, quickly and consistently.
21. Extended Scenario Drills
Drill 1 - Situation
Supplier sends cheaper cheese but the flavour is different.

Best answer
Do not change automatically. Report to Head Chef. Test quality if asked. Consider cost, taste, consistency and customer expectation.

Drill 2 - Situation
A customer asks for vegan pasta but the sauce contains butter.

Best answer
Inform Head Chef/FOH, offer a vegan sauce if available, use clean pan/utensils and avoid cheese/butter/cream.

Drill 3 - Situation
Prep list says 40 portions but bookings suddenly increase.

Best answer
Inform chef, check stock, prioritise high-demand items and prepare extra according to par level and SRC.

Drill 4 - Situation
A dish sells poorly for two weeks.

Best answer
Report feedback, check presentation/quality, review sales records and suggest improvements to Head Chef.

Drill 5 - Situation
You notice staff are over-portioning chips.

Best answer
Remind them to follow SRC and use correct scoop/basket; report repeated issue to supervisor.

Drill 6 - Situation
The Head Chef asks you to calculate chicken portions for 80 guests at 180 g each.

Best answer
80 x 180 g = 14,400 g = 14.4 kg. Add a small buffer if required by chef.

Drill 7 - Situation
A Muslim customer asks whether a sauce contains alcohol.

Best answer
Check recipe/label, ask chef if unsure and communicate clearly through FOH. Do not guess.

Drill 8 - Situation
A customer says they are lactose intolerant but wants mashed potato.

Best answer
Check whether mash contains milk/butter/cream. Offer lactose-free or alternative side if available.

22. Speaking Shadowing Lines
I assist my Head Chef by checking stock, controlling portions and giving practical feedback.
I always follow the Standard Recipe Card to maintain consistency and control food cost.
I use scales, ladles and measuring cups to keep portions consistent.
I do not change ingredients without approval from the Head Chef.
If a customer has an allergy, I check ingredients carefully and avoid cross-contact.
I reduce waste by using FIFO, storing food correctly and preparing the right quantity.
Par stock helps the kitchen avoid over-ordering and running out of ingredients.
Seasonal ingredients are often fresher, more available and better value.
For buffet planning, I calculate the number of guests multiplied by the portion size.
If I am not sure about a dietary request, I stop and ask. I never guess.

Vietnamese note: Trả lời theo cấu trúc: định nghĩa + quy trình tại bếp + lý do an toàn/chất lượng + ví dụ thực tế.

Follow-up Strategy
Item
Details
Definition
Give meaning, purpose and example.
Procedure
Answer step by step in order.
Temperature
Give exact number, thermometer and corrective action.
Scenario
Stop, make safe, report, record, correct.
Dish process
Mise en place, cooking method, temperature, plating.

Answer question 100 aloud in 60 seconds.
Answer question 150 aloud in 60 seconds.
Answer question 200 aloud in 60 seconds.
Answer question 250 aloud in 60 seconds.
Answer question 300 aloud in 60 seconds.

Final Revision Checklist
I can answer the main questions in this book in 45-60 seconds.
I can explain the key procedures without reading.
I know the important vocabulary and can pronounce it clearly.
I can give one real example from my workplace.
I can answer common follow-up questions calmly.

STANDARD FULL VERSION | SAME FORM ACROSS THE WHOLE SET

Item
Details
Purpose
Skills Assessment Cook interview preparation
Language
English model answers + Vietnamese translation and explanation
Form
Cover, table of contents, core knowledge, interview cards, vocabulary, mistakes, tips, mini tests, study plan
Main focus
Mock interviews, scoring rubric, final revision and speaking practice
Use
Study one book at a time, then practise with Book 7 and Book 8


Standard Learning Form
Section
Purpose
1. Topic Overview
Know what the assessor can test.
2. Core Knowledge
Learn key definitions, procedures, temperatures and safety rules.
3. Interview Cards
Practise model answers and Vietnamese translation.
4. Vocabulary + IPA
Review kitchen English and pronunciation.
5. Follow-up Questions
Prepare for deeper assessor questions.
6. Common Mistakes
Avoid typical Vietnamese candidate mistakes.
7. Assessor Tips
Answer like a real cook, not like a textbook.
8. Mini Test + Study Plan
Use active recall and speaking practice.


How to Use This Book
Scoring Rubric
50 Mock Interviews
Answer Strategy
Final 7-Day Plan

How to Use This Book
Item
Details
Step 1
Choose one mock test.
Step 2
Answer aloud without notes.
Step 3
Score each question from 1 to 5.
Step 4
Revise weak topics in Books 1-7.

Scoring Rubric
Score
Meaning
5
Correct, clear, safe, practical.
4
Good answer, minor grammar issue.
3
Understandable but lacks detail.
2
Too short or uncertain.
1
Wrong or unsafe.

50 Mock Interviews
Mock Interview 1
1. Fifo? Nếu bạn nhìn vào shelf or cool room / containers làm sao bạn nhận ra được ?
2. What are personal hazards? (các mối nguy hiểm cá nhân)
3. What methods are used to control par stock?
4. How do you clean the kitchen bench? What do you use to clean?
5. What is an emulsion?
6. What dishes do you cook?
Score: ____ / 30    Weak topic: __________________________

5. If the good is at 7oC when receiving, should you put it outside or put in the cool
