KITCHEN  OPERATION
1. What do you do when you start your shift?
Check temperature of all fridges and freezers; check preparation list from previous shift; check bookings and orders for the day; mise en place for service.
2. What do you do when you end your shift?
Clean all equipment and work surfaces; put leftover ingredients in storage/fridge; write preparation list for the next shift.
3. If you know that tomorrow is a busy day, what will you do?
Check the stock (stocktaking) in dry store and freezer; order more if there is not enough stock; mise en place for the shift tomorrow.
4. What do you do when you receive delivery?
	•	inspect the packaging of the delivery for any damage, sign of pests
	•	check the quantity; temperature, colour, texture of the goods
	•	inspect the condition of the delivery truck and driver
5. Why do you carry out stocktake?
	•	to prevent missing stock or theft
	•	to ensure correct stock rotation (FIFO)
	•	to check any damage or pests
	•	to ensure the goods are stored in proper conditions
6. What are the food safety document?
	•	Temperature log of fridge, freezer, dry store
	•	Temperature log of delivered stock, stock in freezer, cooked food
	•	Record of handling food in food danger zone (2 and 4 hour rule)
7. If a machine breaks down, what will you do?
	•	Unplug the machine,
	•	Tell colleagues in the kitchen to stop using the machine,
	•	Put up a warning sign,
	•	Report to head chef/supervisor or contact to the technician directly,
	•	If possible, remove the machine from the kitchen, put it in a secure place or lock room.
8. How do you ensure the kitchen runs smoothly?
	•	Develop good working relationships with colleagues,
	•	Prepare working plan for service time,
	•	Prepare prep list before each shift,
	•	Provide support to colleagues if they need help
9. How do you deal with conflicts in the kitchen?
	•	Have a private face-to-face conversation with colleagues,
	•	Each party tells their side of the story,
	•	Both parties work on solving the misunderstanding/problem,
	•	Be attentive and respectful to the other party.
10. What do you do when there is a fire in your kitchen?
	•	Do not panic, keep calm,
	•	Turn off the gas supply and electricity,
	•	Get a wet towel and apron,
	•	Walk to the nearest exit door, 
	•	Do not use elevator and stay away glass window or glass materials,
	•	Call Fire Department / Fire-fighters.
11. How do you create a new menu?
	•	Check the restaurant selling record to identify customer needs,
	•	Understand the seasonal foods and festival seasons of a year to choose proper ingredients,
	•	Ensure to maintain a balanced and diverse menu for plenty of choices,
	•	Carry out stocktake to use up any slow-moving stock.
12. What does sustainability mean to you?
Sustainability is: to use the Earth's resources to live without affecting the next generation's ability to live healthily and peacefully.
13. How do you work sustainably in the kitchen?
	•	Recycle leftover food
	•	Do not order too much or make overproduction of food,
	•	Ensure correct storage condition,
	•	Turn off equipment when not in use,
	•	Reduce, reuse and recycle.
14. How do you clean the fridge?
Remove all food first; then remove drawers; use sanitizer to clean them (mix sanitizer with water as instruction of package)
15. When you put leftover food, what do you do if vegetable perished?
I will throw it away. It's not good or dangerous to use perished vegetable.
16. What do you do if you see a smelly chicken in the fridge?
I will throw it away immediately. It could have lots of bacteria to cause food poisoning.
17. Why raw chicken is more dangerous than raw beef?
There is higher risk of salmonella and E-coli in raw chicken than raw beef.
18. How many maximum guests do you serve a day?
It's about/around/approximately 150 per day and nearly 300 on a busy day.
19. How long your customer get the dish after they order?
It depends on the type of dish. It should be from 5 minutes to 15 minutes.
20. How do you order stock?
In my restaurant, Chef de Partie will do that. In case, it's my duty, I will check the numbers of guest booking, any special requirments, and the stock to make a suitable order.  
21. What do you do if a guest return the dish back? He/She doesn't eat.
I will find out the reason and make a new dish for him or her. It should be for free it is our fault.
22. Which section do you work in the kitchen?
Most of the time I work in hot kitchen. But i also make salad and prepare vegetable when needed.
23. Where do you keep chemical?
I keep it in bottle or container with label in separate section.
24. What are the sources of chemical contamination?
	•	Fertilisers or insecticides not being washed off fruits and vegetables before processing,
	•	Cleaning chemicals getting into or onto food because of poor storage,
	•	Incorrect use of chemicals can be transferred onto food from cooking or preparation equipment.
25. How to avoid chemical contamination?
	•	Wash fruit and vegetable before use them,
	•	Ensure correct storage of food, correct labelling of chemicals,
	•	Staff need to be trained on how to use chemicals correctly.
26. What do you do when using the blender and it's broken?
	•	Unplug it,
	•	Label warning sign,
	•	Remove it to separate place and call technician to fix it.
27. If you have a wet floor, how you solve it?
	•	Stop people from accessing wet area,
	•	Put a warning sign,
	•	Clean and dry the floor quickly
28. What is the background colour of the wet floor warning sign?
Yellow and black
29. Why not red?
Red is for fire and danger
30. What temperature you keep your salad?
From 3 degree C to 5 degree C in chiller or fridge.
31. What the first things should you do if a pan catches fire?
	•	Turn off the stove, (do not pour water over the burning grease)
	•	Cover the fire with the pan lid (not glass lid), or backing soda, or a thick damp towel over the flame (not wet towel)
32. If you see a big fire in a building next to your kitchen, what will you do?
	•	I will turn off electricity and gas,
	•	Make an arlam for everyone,
	•	Walk to the nearest exit,
	•	Call Fire Department if possible.
33. What do you do when you evacuate?
Walk to the essembling point.
34. Do you walk near the window?
No. It's dangerous. Fire can break the glass window to hurt anyone near it. 
35. Will you use an elevator? Why?
No, i won't. Fire can turn off the elevator and I can get stuck in there.
36. Why should you go to assembly point?
It's a safety place where people have been told to gather and wait after evacuating for an appropriate space to headcount. It also allows people to calm down from stressful procedure and for anyone who needs medical treatment.
37. If your colleage still gets stuck in the building. Will you come back to help him/her?
No, it's dangerous. I will call the Fire Department for help.
38. What is the Fire Department phone number in Vietnam and Australia?
114 in Vietnam. 
000 in Australia.
39. What is solar power?
Solar power is the conversion of energy from sunlight into electricity, either directly or indirectly or a combination.
40. How many types of garbage do you have?
There are 5 types: liquid waste; solid rubbish; organic waste; recyclable rubbish; and hazardous waste.
Some places only have 2 types: organic and recycle.
41. How often do you change the oil of deep fryer?
It depends on how often we cook and the colour of oil is turned dark brown or thick. We use a filter to remove unwanted stains from the old oil and change it.
42. When you throw away oil, what do you do?
Cool down the cooking oil; if the oil is a little, wipe it up with a paper towel; if the oil is pretty much, pour it in a container, then seal it and toss it in the trash.
43. How do you train a staff?
	•	Prepare a suitable time and session for coaching/training. (not at peak/busy time),
	•	Arrange "on the job training" to teach specific tasks and technical skills in practices; also arrange job rotation to help staff more flexible in different sections,
	•	Attend meetings to gain knowledge how the business is operated and running,
	•	Create a positive environment to encourage new staff learning and working well.
44. What if new staff don't follow the rules?
As a supervisor, I would have a face-to-face communication to know the reason to solve the problem. Next, I would give them a second chance to be trained again with another suitable procedure. 
If the issue continues happening, I would report to our Boss/Director to have a decision.

APPETIZER
45. Give examples of appetizers (salads, soups, chicken, seafood, beef dishes)?
	•	Salad: Caesar salad, Greek salad, Russian salad, Thai beef salad, Garden salad...
	•	Soup: consomme (clear), pumpkin soup (puree),  French onion soup, gazpacho (cold), clam chowder, lobster bisque....
	•	Chicken: chicken skewers, chicken kebab, honey and soy sauce chicken wings,.....
	•	Beef: mini beef burger, beef tartare, beef lollipop,...
	•	Seafood: seafood terrine, smoked salmon rolled sandwich, shrimp cocktail, oyster ceviche,....
46. Give examples of salad dressing?
	•	Mayonnaise-based: Thousand island dressing, Caesar dressing, Cocktail dressing,
	•	Vinaigrette-based: Italian dressing, mustard dressing, lemon dressing,...
47. Can you serve potato soup cold?
Yes, it depends on the seasons and guests.
48. Name  cold soups?
Avocado soup, cucumber soup, tomato basil soup, gapazcho soup, green pea soup, Greek yoghurt soup,.. ...
49. What is chowder? Give examples.
Chowder is a thick soup with milk or cream, roux and seafood or vegetable. 
Potato chowder, clam chowder, seafood chowder, corn chowder,....

STOCKS AND SAUCES
50. How do you make brown beef stock?
	•	Roast bones and mirepoix until bones turn brown all sides.
	•	Transfer bones and mirepoix to a large stock pot and bring mixture to a boil. 
	•	Reduce heat to simmer, uncovered, for 3 hours. 
	•	Remove from heat and let cool to lukewarm.
	•	Strain stock, reserving liquid. Discard bones, mirepoix, and spices. 
	•	Place stock liquid in a container; cover and refrigerate until thoroughly chilled. 
	•	Lift solid fat from top of stock; discard fat. 
Note: Use Basic Brown Stock as a base for soups, sauces, or gravies. Basic Brown Stock may be frozen up to 3 months.
51. When you make stock, do you let the water boil or simmer? Why?
Simmer. 
Stocks are simmered so the fat and proteins released by any cooking meat or bones float to the top, where they can be skimmed off instead of making the stock cloudy and greasy.
52. Name the base sauces?
	•	Bechamel: white roux + milk
	•	Veloute: blonde roux + white stock
	•	Brown sauce / demi glace: brown roux + brown stock
	•	Tomato sauce; Hollandaise; Mayonnaise
53. What is the difference between bechamel and veloute?
Bechamel sauce uses milk
Veloute uses stock
54. How do you fix a Mayonnaise that is separating?
Add some cold water and whisk until the mixture emulsifies.
55. What is the reason for splitting Hollandise sauce?
Pouring too much oil at once, not whisking fast enough, incorrect temperature.

POULTRY
56. What do you stuff inside a chicken?
Roasted chicken can be stuffed with chunky carrot, onion, celery, garlic, crushed peppercorns,...
57. Can you stuff breadcrumbs inside chicken?
Yes
58. What method you can cook with turkey?
Roast; stew; braise
59. How do you cook quail?
Pan fry it with garlic butter
60. Which stock longest cooking time among beef stock, chicken, and fish stock?
Beef stock
61. What cooking method else with chicken?
Roast, stew, braise, fry, deep fry
62. What kinds of poultry do you cook?
Chicken, Duck, Turkey, Quail
63. What is the difference between roasting a chicken and a turkey?
Turkey is roasted at lower temperature (160 -170 C) with longer time than chicken.
64. What do you stuff inside a turkey?
Herbs, nut, chestnut, citrus, breadcrumbs and served with cranberry sauce
65. How many parts would you cut a chicken?
10 parts: 2 breasts, 2 thighs, 2 wings, 2 drumsticks, 1 part of back portion and a tail end of back portion.
66. Different sauces that you can serve with chicken?
	•	Peppercorn sauce, mushroom sauce, 
	•	Diane sauce, white wine sauce,
	•	Orange sauce, cranberry sauce

EGGS
67. Name some egg dishes?
	•	Omelete, scrambled egg, frittata, quiche Loraine
	•	Egg benedict, egg florentine
68. What is the difference between frittata and quiche loraine?
Frittata does not have a short pastry crust.

SEAFOOD
69. About fish. How many fillets do you cut a flat fish?
Four pieces
70. How about round fish?
Two pieces
71. What kind of fish for deep-frying?
Lean fish (such as: sea bass) or shellfish is suitable and must be coated before deep frying.
Fat fish is not good for deep frying.
72. What method of cooking for mussels?
Steam, grill with cheese, chowder...
73. How do you remove sand from mussels?
Soak them in clean water
74. Do you add salt?
NO, they are salty enough as they are from the sea
75. How do you make colour for lobster bisque?
I use lobster head, shell and tomato paste
76. How do you cook pork?
Braise, roast, smoke, bake.... It depends on which part or cut of pork.
77. What sauce with?
Creamy mustard sauce, BBQ sauce, mushroom sauce, honey-garlic sauce, horseradish sauce....
78. What types of seafood do you cook?
	•	Fish: salt water fish (sea bass, tuna...); fresh water fish (catfish, carp, red tilapia...); flat fish (sole, halibut); round fish (red snapper); fat fish (salmon, mackerel); lean fish (red snapper, sea bass)
	•	Shellfish: mollusc (clam, mussel, oyster, scallop, squid, octopus); crustacean (crab, prawn, shrimp, lobster)
79. What is the cooking techniques suitable for seafood?
Poach, grill, pan-sear
80. How do you cook mussels?
Steam, grill, chowder, pan fry
81. How do you know the mussels are overcooked?
The flesh is deflated, tough, no moisture
82. What temperature should fish be cooked?
The internal temperature should be 63ºC to kill harmful bacteria, but some chefs recommend cooking certain types of seafood or fish a little below this to keep it moist.
For example: 
White fish, such as cod and monkfish: 52ºC 
Halibut: 50ºC 
Flatfish, Salmon: 52ºC 
Tuna: 31ºC  (yields a raw center)
Eel: 70ºC 
83. How do you cook lobsters?
We can boil, steam, or grill lobsters.
For boiling:
	•	Freezing lobsters for about 30 minutes,
	•	Boiling lobsters in salted water (like sea water), cover tightly, for 10- 15 minnutes, depending on the size of them (lobsters should be a bright vivid red color when done),
	•	Drain lobsters to cool down slightly before plating.
84. What kind of fish is suitable  for poaching?
Poaching is a good technique for many kinds of fish, such as: lean fish like tilapia, cod, sole, snapper, or halibut, as well as fatty fish like: salmon or trout. 
85. In general, why do we poach fish rather than boil fish?
Poaching is suited to the fine and delicated texture of fish. It also enhances the flavour of fish. 
Boiling will cause the flesh to break up.
86. How do you drop the fish into the deep-fryer?
	•	Drop one end of the fillet first,
	•	Hold the other end for a few seconds before dropping it,
	•	The fillet will float to the surface when thoroughly cooked.
87. Types of deep-frying batters?
	•	Tempura batter: flour, corn flour, baking powder, soda water.
	•	Yeast batter: yeast, warm water, flour.
	•	Beer batter: flour, salt, beer.
88. What are different sauces that you can serve with fish?
	•	Beurre blanc, hollandiase, bearnaise
	•	Peppercorn sauce, white wine sauce

MEAT
89. How do you clarify beef consommé?
	•	Remove the sinews and gristle from the beef and chop it into smaller pieces.
	•	Dice the vegetables/mirepoix and put them into a saucepan with meat and egg white.
	•	Pour in the stock and bring to a boil, mixing constantly.
	•	Turn the heat to the lowest setting and simmer for 1 and a half hours.
	•	Remove the fat from the surface and strain the consommé.
90. Name some types of appertizer with beef?
Beef tartar, mini burger, beef skewers, roast feef Bruschetta, cocktail meatballs,....
91. Name chicken appertizers?
Greek chicken meatballs, buffalo chicken wings, red curry chicken fingers, satay with peanut sauce skewers, chicken & black bean nachos,....
92. If the chicken come to your kitchen at 10 degree C. Do you receive or not?
No. I will send it back to the supplier and require another chicken at right temperature (0ºC - 5ºC) and standard quality for my kitchen.
93. What is the difference between baking and roasting method?
Temperature: Roasting happens at higher temperatures (from 205ºC) than baking. Baking is done at lower oven temperatures, below and up to 200ºC. Because of the higher heat, roasting results in shorter cook times and makes the outside of the food crispy.
Types of food: Cooking meats or vegetables is considered roasting because the food already has a solid structure. Baked foods are typically not solid before they are cooked. Instead, baked foods typically go from liquid or dough to solid food during the cooking process.
Methods: Baking almost always occurs in an oven. Roasting can use an oven but you can also technically roast something over an open flame or in a closed barbeque.
94. Name types of appertizer with pork?
Pulled pork bites, pork meatballs with hoisin sauce, crispy pork buns, bacon wrapped Bratwurst bites,.......
95. What cuts of beef do you cook?
	•	Tenderloin, Rib-eye, T-bone, sirloin, rum, flank can be grilled.
	•	Chuck, neck, brisket, shank can be roasted or braised.
	•	Bone, tail can be cooked for stock.
96. Which part of the cow is the ribeye steak?
It's a cut out meat taken from between the sixth and twelfth ribs of the cow. 
97. What types of meat do the T-bone steak have?
Sirloin, tenderloin, strip
98. How do you check the doneness of steak?
Finger touch to feel the tenderness,
Use thermometer to check the internal temperature.
99. Why do we have to rest the steak before serving?
Resting the steak long enough aims to retain the juices that you lose when cutting into the meat. It also helps the meat tender, tasty and juicy.
100. If we put the thermometer inside the steak to check the temperature, there will be a hole and juices will come out. How do we prevent that?
Put it back on the pan and sear the hole to seal it.
101. Describe the steps in braising beef?
	•	Season beef with salt, pepper and Worcestershire sauce.
	•	Sear beef with vegetable oil and brown sugar. Turn until it’s golden on every side.
	•	Add vegetables and some Bay leaves. Cook for about 5 minutes then low heat to medium.
	•	Add beef stock, red wine, tomato puree, cover and cook for approximately 2 hours at low heat.
	•	Remove Bay leaves. Blend the vegetables until smooth to make sauce. Cut the beef and serve with that sauce.
102. What sauces would you serve with steak?
Mushroom sauce, peppercorn sauce, red white sauce, BBQ sauce, 
Garlic parmesan cream sauce, chimichurri sauce, creamy mustard sauce,.....
103. What cuts of goat do you use for curry?
Tough cuts like neck, shoulder, leg, shank

SPECIAL DIETS 
104. Give examples of special diets?
	•	Celiac: gluten-free diet, no wheat flour. Replace with corn flour, tapioca, rice flour.
	•	Lactose intolerance: lactose-free diet, lactose-free foods are dairy products where the lactose has been removed. (A lactose-free product is not necessarily dairy-free.)
	•	Dairy allergy: dairy-free diet, no casien or whey protein. A dairy-free product must have no milk and no ingredients that are part of milk at all
	•	Diabetes: Avoid high-fat dairy products and animal proteins (butter, beef, hot dogs, sausage and bacon);  Avoid trans fats in processed foods, baked goods, shortening and stick margarines; Avoid unhealthy carbohydrates (foods or drinks with added fats, sugars and sodium).
	•	Vegan: no animal products strictly, avoids consuming dairy, eggs, and any other ingredients derived from animals.
	•	Vegetarian: no meat but can consume dairy, eggs, and any other ingredients derived from animals.
	•	Muslim: no pork and all products from pork/big, including gelatin; no alcohol; Halal food 
	•	Jewish: no pork strictly; no shellfish at all; only certain fish which have fins and scales are permitted (cod, haddock and bream) ; no birds of prey (only eat chicken, turkey, duck and goose) ; Kosher food
105. What can replace gelatine for a vegan?
Agar agar, pectin from fruit and vegetable
106. Design one dessert for diabetes?
Orange cheese cake with low-carb and low-fat ingredients; 
Or sorbet.

PASTRY
109. Name some different types of pastries?
	•	Choux pastry: cream puff, eclair, profiterole
	•	Puff pastry: beef Wellington crust, strudel, pate chaud
	•	Shortcrust pastry: quiche Lorraine, meat pie
	•	Sweet pastry: fruit tart, chocolate tart, apple pie
110. How do you make choux pastry?
	•	Sieve the flour and salt 3 times to make sure it is very light. 
	•	Heat butter, water, and milk together, until the butter melts, 
	•	When the water is boiling fast immediately tip all the flour into the water and butter mixture. Turn off the heat. 
	•	Beat the mixture with a wooden spoon until all the lumps of flour have gone. The dough should smell very fragrant once it's done and change color from very pale white to a more pale tan,
	•	Place this mixture, which is called a panade, on a plate to cool. Cover with cling film so that it doesn’t form a skin. Leave it for 15 minutes or until cold.
	•	Put it back in the pan and start to add the beaten eggs a little at a time, beating well.
	•	Pipe small choux buns onto a parchment lined baking sheet. 
	•	Put the choux buns in a preheated oven 200ºC with Fan. Bake for 25 – 35 minutes, depending on the size of the choux buns.
	•	Take the choux pastry out of the oven, turn them over and put a hole  into the bases. Place them upside down and put in the oven for another 5 minutes. This helps the steam to escape and stops the buns becoming soggy.
111. What is the temperature for baking puff pastry?
200ºC to 220ºC

BREAD and CAKE
112. What are the main ingredients for bread?
Flour, water, yeast and salt.
113. How do you know the bread is ready for baking?
Press your fingers into the dough, if it slowly springs back, it's ready to bake.
114. How many times do you let the bread dough rise?
2 times: one after mixing the dough, and one after divide into individual shapes.
115. How do you know the bread is done?
	•	Tap to check, if it sounds hollow then it's done; 
	•	Look for a golden brown appearance. 
Checking with a thermometer is the best way for soft bread or cake at 88ºC to 99ºC 
116. Give examples of frozen / gelatine / custard-based / fruit desserts?
	•	Frozen: Ice cream, sorbet
	•	Custard: creme caramel, creme brulee
	•	Gelatine: panna cotta, mousse
	•	Fruit: poached pear, banana flambe, fruit tart.
117. What is the difference between ice cream and sorbet?
	•	Ice cream contains cream and eggs.
	•	Sorbet contains only fruit puree and sugar syrup.
118. What is the difference between a sponge cake and a cake?
	•	A sponge cake uses whipped eggs as the raising agent , causing the sponge to rise (without baking soda or powder) by whisking egg white method to trap the air into the mixture.
	•	A cake uses baking powder to rise and it also contains high amount of fat.
119. What can we serve together with cake?
	•	Chocolate sauce, fruit coulis,
	•	Whipped cream, butter-cream frosting, cream cheese frosting,
	•	Nuts, fresh fruits, ice cream. 
120. Do you serve a pudding warm or cold?
It can be served either warm or cold, depends on the season of the year and the preference. 
121. What temperature do you bake bread?
The ideal bread-baking temperatures range from 180ºC to 250ºC  and depends on the kind of bread.
122. Give some examples of pastry without yeast.
	•	Choux pastry: éclair, profiterole, cream puff.
	•	Shortcrust pastry: quiche Lorraine, meat pie.
	•	Puff pastry: beef Wellington, strudel.
123. What is éclair?
An éclair is a long French pastry made from choux pastry, filled with pastry cream or custard and topped with chocolate fondant.
124. How do you make quiche Lorraine?
	•	Make the dough; shape in moulds; blind baking,
	•	Chop bacon, onion into small pieces and stir fry them,
	•	Fill the bacon mixture into the baked tart,
	•	Mix cheese and whipping cream in well beaten eggs to pour in the tart,
	•	Bake in the oven at 190ºC  with fan for about 25 mins, or until golden and softly set.
125. When you make tart, you need to blind baking. Explain it?
Blind baking means prebaking the crust 80% first before adding the filling and baking again until done. 
126. When you make apple tart, what do you put on top when blind baking?
Raw black bean.
127. How do you make beef Wellington?
	•	Season a tenderloin with salt and pepper, then sear it until well-browned on all sides, including the ends. Let it chill in the fridge.
	•	Stir-fry chopped mushroom, onion, garlic minced, 
	•	Lay some slices prosciutto on the cling film, spread the mushroom duxelles over the prosciutto, then sit the tenderloin on it, then roll it into a sausage shape, twisting the ends of cling film to tighten it, chill it in fridge.
	•	Unwrap the cling film and put the whole tenderloin roll in the center of a puff pastry sheet, brush the edges of the pastry with egg wash, then tightly wrap the tenderloin into the pastry.
	•	Brush the Wellington with a little  egg yolk and bake in the oven at 200ºC  with fan, until golden and crisp (Note: 20-25 mins for medium-rare beef ; 30 mins for medium). 
	•	Allow to stand for 10 mins before serving in thick slices.
128. Do you cook spinach before you add into tart?
Yes, spinach needs to be removed water by cooking first before add into tart. (to prevent water make the dough soggy)  
129. What temperature do you bake sweet pastry?
Sweet pastry contains sugar to be burnt easily, so the temperature should be low at 180ºC  - 190ºC. 
130. What is filling of choux pastry?
It can be whipped cream, custard, or pastry cream.
131. What is profiterole?
It is choux pastry ball with a typically sweet and moist filling of whipped cream, custard, or pastry cream and garnished with chocolate sauce, caramel, or a dusting of powdered sugar.
132. What is the basic technique difference between Croissant and Danish?
Croissants and Danish pastries are both multilayered and laminated. 
	•	But Croissant dough is believed to have more layers when baked. It contains yeast, which gives the dough a lighter and softer texture, and helps it rise.
	•	Danish dough is often added eggs because the dough needs to be sturdier to add fillings. 
133. How to make muffin?
	•	Preheat oven 190ºC .
	•	Butter muffin cups/pans/moulds. 
	•	Mix the flour, baking powder, salt, and sugar in a large bowl. 
	•	Add the egg, milk, and butter, stirring only enough to dampen the dry ingredients; the batter should not be smooth. 
	•	Spoon into the muffin cups/pans/moulds, filling each one about two-thirds full. 
	•	Bake for about 20 to 25 minutes each.
134. What is meringue?
	It's a whipped white egg and sugar combination.
135. When you make shortcrust pastry, what method you use? Rubbing or creaming?
	Use creaming method. This method avoids flour and water contact.
136. If you have ingredients: sugar, butter, flour, egg. What order do you mix them together?
	Fat and sugar, then add egg, and flour is the last one. Flour is for folding all 	of them.
137. How do you make puff pastry?
	•	Make the dough first (including butter, flour, water and salt). 
	•	Put soft butter in the center of the dough, cover the edge and press the dough with a rolling pin to make it longer. 
	•	Fold it into 3 parts and roll it again. Repeat 5 to 6 times. Make sure the butter stays cool, refrigerate the dough for 30 minutes every two turns — or between every turn if your kitchen is very warm.
138. How do you make pastry puff  up?
Before baking, the pastry should be cold and thawed, the oven should be preheat at proper temperature (190ºC  - 200ºC  ).
During baking, the fat turns to oil to separate the dough into layers, meanwhile, water turns to steaming to help pastry raise.
139. How do you make strudel?
Strudel is made with strudel dough which is stretched into a soft, long and thin sheet. We can add many different fillings, such as: meat, mushroom, fruits (apple, cherry, pear, apricot...), nut (almond...).
140. How do you decorate a rice pudding?
Raisin, berries, fresh fruits, diced apples, mango slices,......
141. How do you decorate a birthday cake?
With ganache, fruits, berries, chocolate chips, frosting, nuts, fondant,......

FOOD SAFETY IN THE WORKPLACE
142. What do you know about "The Food Standards Code" in Australia?
It contains standards such as: 
	•	Times for chilling food after cooking,
	•	Training and education standards for food handlers.
143. If an area has been contaminated by pests, what would you do?
The whole area needs to be cleaned and sanitised before it can be used as a food area again.
144. What is the temperature danger zone for food poisoning? And why?
The danger zone is between 5ºC  and 60ºC . 
Most bacteria will survive and grow rapidly to large number in that Danger Zone.
145. What temperature can kill bacteria?
Most bacteria are killed when you cook or heat food to above 75ºC  .
146. What kinds of food need to be stored between OºC   to 5ºC ?
High risk food such as: milk, raw meat, poultry, eggs, cooked meat, ready-to-eat food and fish.
147. Will you re-frozen any thawed food? Why?
No. Because food poisoning bacteria may grow to large number and the quality of food will deterioate.
148. What temperature in the center has cooked food reached?
It's need to be at least 75ºC .
149. Can we mix different chemicals to clean and sanitise the kitchen?
Never. It's dangerous.
150. Give some examples of physical contamination may include?
Glass, hair, finger nails, insects, metal shavings, wood shavings,....



ADDITIONAL REAL QUESTIONS IN PREVIOUS SKILL ASSESSMENT EXAMS

151.  What is 2 hour - 4 hour rule during cooling down food process? (How do you control the cooked food not expose to the danger zone?)
	It is the general rule for handling and stores or perishable food. If any ready-to-eat food has been at between 5ºC  and 60ºC , it must follow the rule:
	•	less than 2 hours: must be refrigerated or used immediately.
	•	longer than 2 hours but less than 4 hours: must be used immediately
	•	from 4 hours or longer: must be thrown out.
152.  What do you do if the kitchen floor get wet or slippery (spilled water or oil)?
	•	Put a slippery caution sign at the wet area;
	•	Clean the area immediately with proper mop (use dry mop if water spilled ; if oil spilled, spinkle salt over the oil for a minute, wipe it with dry towel, mop it with soapy water and use dry mop to clean it again);
	•	Remove the caution sign when make sure the area is totally dry.
153.  How to prevent an acident in your kitchen?
	•	Keep safety around the Stove or Heat area: be aware of the heat from an empty burner after it is turned off;  set a timer to remind you when food is ready; keep anything flammable such as napkins, towels, cloths, plastics, and paper products away from the range or stove; use dry oven mitts or pot holders ; when lifting lids, direct steam away from yourself; avoid storing things on top of appliances or stove ;  never pour water on a pan fire involving grease; 
	•	Knife Safety: wash knives separately from other items — never place a knife in a full of water sink; do not try to catch a falling knife ; have a separate storage area for knives from other utensils ; keep knife blades and grips clean ; using a sharp knife is safer then a blunt knife ; 
	•	Avoid falls, strains, and bumps: cleaning up messes and spills immediately ; use a sturdy step ladder or step stool to reach for those high up things ; be careful when carrying hot liquids and make sure the path is clear ; proper lifting techniques prevent back injuries ; never lift more than you are capable of ; keep cabinet doors and drawers closed to avoid bumps and pinched fingers; use the proper uniform and slip-resistant footwear.

	(The answer depends on the real experience of each candidate)
156.	How do you look after food safety? (What do you do for food safety?). Give an example.
	•	Follow hygiene procedures and identify food hazards.
	•	Report any personal health issues.
	•	Prevent food contamination.
	•	Prevent cross contamination by washing hands.
157.	How do you ensure nobody get sick when you produce food for people? (What rules do you follow to make sure noone or customers get sick when you produce food for them?)
	•	Clean — Wash hands and surfaces often, wash utensils and equipment properly.
	•	Separate and label (raw food, cooked food and chemical) — Prevent cross-contaminate.
	•	Cook — Cook to proper temperatures, checking with a food thermometer.
	•	Chill — Refrigerate promptly, follow the 2 hour-4hour rule strictly.
158.	What do you know about cooking temperature for food safety? Give examples.
In general, food should be cooked to a temperature of at least 75°C or hotter. When food is cooked, it should be eaten promptly, kept hotter than 60°C, or cooled, covered and stored in the fridge or freezer.
The internal temperature should be:
	•	Fish:  63ºC
	•	Minced meat, sausages:  71ºC
	•	Beef, veal, lamb, pork: 
		*medium rare:  63ºC - allow at least 3 minutes for the meat to rest
		*medium:  71ºC
		*well done: 77ºC
	•	Ham: fresh (raw) : 71ºC;  precooked (to reheat): 60ºC
	•	Chicken and turkey (whole), thighs, wings legs, breasts: 74ºC
	•	Leftovers: 75ºC
	•	Most cakes:  around 98°C
	•	Bread: 
		*White bread, Sandwich loaves, Bread rolls, Buns, Whole-Grain rounds,..... : 88ºC - 93ºC (depends on the weight, time and type of the oven)
		*Sourdough, No-Knead Bread, Banana bread,..... : 96ºC  - 99ºC.	
159.	What is the difference between artisan bread and white bread?
	Artisan bread: is handmade with natural ingredients ; with high quality but small quantity ; does not contain preservatives ; requires a long fermentation time ; contains more gluten and yeast ; has more air pockets and moisture ; and uses traditional techniques.
	White bread: Most white bread is made using modern techniques to lower production costs ; follows a rapid production technique ; is made with white wheat flour ; has a fine texture and fewer nutrients ; is a processed food with low-quality carbs and can contain added sugar.
160. Compare fermented dough and instant dough?
	Fermented dough is used to make fermented bread which is contained a variety of yeasts and bacteria that help to convert the important nutrients and sugars in the dough into a form that is more readily digestible than instant yeast breads. 
	Instant dough is made from yeast which is usually sold in packets and called fast-rising or instant yeast. It uses a single strain of specially cultured yeast for making quick-rising breads. 
	The taste, texture and overall character of fermented dough are often markedly different than that of instant dough. 
161.	The texture inside the bread is changed when we make with instant dough and when we make it with fermented dough. How does it change?


162.	What would you act if the supplier provides meat at inappropriate temperature?
	•	Return the meat to the supplier, let them know the reason and require to change the proper ones;
	•	Report to the supervisor or chef or boss;
	•	If it happens more than 2 or 3 times, we should change another supplier who follows the food safety.
163.	How does oil and steaming affect on the dough?
	Oil: improve the crumb structure, make a softer and moist loaf because the oil in the dough prevents water from evaporating during baking.
	Steaming: keep the surface of the loaf remain moist and expands easily, also shiny the crust.
164.	Someone gets injured in your kitchen. Who is responsible for the accident? Who pay for the damage? 
	•	If the person/the staff is discipline and follows the kitchen rules, then the restaurant/the company will be responsible and pay through insurance policy.
	•	If the person/the staff is not discipline and does not follow the rules, then he or she will be responsible for all the damage and the injury and pay for it by themselves. 
165.	What kinds of artisan bread do you know?
	•	Baguette
	•	Ciabatta
	•	Focaccia
	•	Naturally leavened multigrain bread
	•	Naturally leavened white bread
	•	Panettone and brioche 
166.	How to make a baguette step by step?
	•	Weigh flour. In a large bowl, combine the starter ingredients (cool water, yeast and flour). Mix everything together to make a soft dough. Cover and let rest at room temperature for about 14 hours; overnight works well.
	•	Mix the soft dough with water, salt and knead everything together to make a soft, smooth dough; the finished dough should stick a bit at the bottom of the bowl.
	•	Place the dough in a lightly greased medium-sized bowl, cover the bowl, and let the dough rest and rise for 45 minutes. Gently deflate the dough and fold its edges into the center, then turn it over in the bowl before letting it rise for an additional 45 minutes, until it's noticeably puffy.
	•	Turn the dough out onto a lightly greased work surface. Gently deflate it, and divide it into three equal pieces.
	•	Round each piece of dough into a rough ball. Cover with greased plastic wrap, and let rest for 15 minutes.
	•	Flatten the dough slightly then fold it in half, sealing the edges with the heel of your hand.
	•	Turn the dough around 180° and repeat: fold, then flatten. Repeat this whole process again.
	•	With the seam side down, gently roll the dough into a log. Taper each end of the log slightly to create the baguette's typical "pointy" end.
	•	Place the logs seam-side down onto a lightly greased or parchment-lined sheet pan or pans; Cover them with lightly greased plastic wrap about 45 minutes to an hour at room temperature.
	•	Preheat oven to 230°C with a cast iron pan on the floor of the oven, or on the lowest rack. Start to heat 350ml water to boiling.
	•	Using a very sharp knife to make 3 to 5 long lengthwise slashes in each baguette.
	•	Load the baguettes into the oven. Pour the boiling water into the cast iron pan, and quickly shut the oven door.
	•	Bake the baguettes for 24 to 28 minutes, or until they're a very deep golden brown. Remove them from the oven and cool them on a rack. 
167.	How would you recycle water in your kitchen?
	•	Waste-water from washing vegetables, fruits, rice ; or cooling-down-boiling water ; or aquarium water can be reused for gardening, lawn and landscape,
	•	A long period storing drinking water becomes murky, and also contaminated by bacteria. This water can be reused for washing utensils or other household chores, like mopping the kitchen floor,
	•	Collecting rain water to reuse for multiple tasks, such as cleaning driveway, veranda, garden, lawn, kitchen floor,....
168.	How do you know to make order for your kitchen? (what indicate you to know how much order to the suppliers?) 
Before placing a food order, the cook/chef or manager must look at current inventory levels of all food and supplies to know exactly what is and is not on the shelves. 
	•	First: Use the production sheets and menus together to determine what is needed 
	•	Second: Inventory the operation to see what is already on hand 
	•	Third: Exclude any items that are needed for the current menu or ordering cycle 
	•	Fourth: Place the order for what is needed that is not already in the operation 
169.	What way do you use to make order? And how do you confirm your orders have delivered to the kitchen?
	•	By email, phone call, text message, ordering system by supplier if any,
	•	Check the storage, the timeline, the date of delivery, compare to the order list, call the supplier, check with staff who is on duty to recieve delivery.......
170.	How would you store the chemical? 
	•	All chemicals must be stored in a safe, secure location. 
	•	Hazardous chemicals must be stored below eye level. 
	•	Do not store chemicals on the floor, window ledges, or balconies. 
	•	Store chemicals away from direct sunlight, sources of heat, and egress pathways.
	•	Keep containers closed unless you are dispensing a chemical or adding to the container.
	•	Label containers, and be sure container is compatible with the chemical
171.	How do you look after your fridge / cool room / chiller / freezer?
	•	Empty each shelf of all items,
	•	Throw away any expired or moldy food,
	•	Completely wipe down the interior/shelves of the refrigerator or freezer,
	•	Sweep the floor if it is walk-in coolroom, or walk-in freezer,
	•	Disinfect with a sanitizer,
	•	(Vacuum the condenser and coils, if possible)
	•	Re-arrange food following FIFO rule.
172.	About production schedule. What do you consider before producing? (What factors influence the production schedule in the kitchen?)
	A production schedule is setting aims at preparing the menu. The factors influence the production schedule:
	•	Menu development
	•	Date of event; Type of event
	•	Number of guests
	•	Standard of service
	•	Recipe development
	•	Ingredient sourcing
	•	Food orders
	•	Staffing requirements
	•	Preparation timeline
	•	Plating diagrams
173.	If there is a cable of an electric machine has faulty (has broken), what will you do to eliminate 100% any risk to prevent other people to use that machine?
	Unplug the machine, report to supervisor and mechanician/technician, put a warning sign on the machine and inform to all colleagues, then put it in a locked room/locked spare-room, keep the key until it gets fixed or replaced.
174. How do you recover your dough? / What happen if you do the recovering process too fast?
To recover a retarded dough from fridge/freezer: 
	•	If from fridge: Let the dough proof at room temperature about 1 hour or until test the dough is ready for baking, (or allow your dough to regain some warmth on the counter for 40 to 60 minutes) and become slightly puffy before shaping it by stretching and folding the dough.
	•	If from freezer, place frozen dough in the fridge for 12-24 hours. When ready to use, place the thawed dough in a greased bread pan, cover, and let it rise on the counter at room temperature for 1-2 hours before using.
*** If we do this step too fast, (bake the retarded dough straight from fridge) the bread will be more density, especially in the very center of loaf (not airy) and is slightly smaller in size. Or the center of loaf still frozen (not thawed totally from freezer), it might be not cooked enough.
175.	What is retarding dough? 
	•	Retarding is a technique that slows the fermentation process of your dough. It is done by proofing bread overnight in the refrigerator since the cold slows down the rise. 
	•	The benefit of retarding dough is including adding flavor and allowing you to bake the bread at a later time.
	•	Retarding dough is also a great way to add an extra dimension of flavor to  breads.
176. What is pan-fry, deep-fry, steam, boil, simmer, roast?
	•	Pan-fry: fry food in pan with a small amount of fat/oil/butter.
	•	Deep-fry: fry food in a deep pan in which the food is completely covered by oil.
	•	Steaming: is a moist-heat method of cooking that works by boiling water which vaporizes into steam. The food is kept separate from the boiling water but comes into direct contact with the hot steam
	•	Boil: is the moist heat method that cooks food in boiling water or other water-based liquids (stock, milk). 
	•	Simmer: is a method of cooking that uses a low heat to gently soften foods below boiling point.
	•	Roast: is a method of cooking that uses hot, dry air to cook food in the oven, but at higher temperatures.
177.	How do you know when the dough is kneaded enough?
	When the dough has been kneaded enough, we can see its appearance smooth and satiny. After kneading the dough for several minutes, press it with your finger. 
	•	If the indentation stays, the dough still needs more work. 
	•	If it springs back slowly to its original shape , your dough is ready to rest.	
178.	If you do not put salt in the dough, what is bread like? (How does a bread look and taste like without salt?) 
	It looks pale (pale brown) and less taste and less flavour and weak structure.
179.	How do you shape / form a loaf of bread?
	By hand, roll out the dough, roll up the dough, pinch the seam, keep the seam closed, final shap the dough as the shape you want, place the dough in a pan if needed.
	If you don't have a bread pan, press the two ends closer together, and place the loaf on a greased baking sheet. It will have the look of artisan bread or a classic French bread.
180.	How does yeast multiply? (What does yeast need to multiply?)
	•	Yeast food (sugar, warm liquid for some breads not using sugar);
	•	Moisture (water, other liquid);
	•	Warmth (warm water, warm liquid) 
181. What is the difference between French bread and normal bread?
	•	French bread tends to be longer and narrower, hard and crusty on the outside, with a light and soft crumb. 
	•	It has a more uniform texture, and texture is usually denser. 
182. How do you secure your storage?
Lock it and only staff on duty to keep the key; use surveillance camera system; hire security agents....
183. What are hazards for food safety?
	•	Biological hazards (bacteria, fungi, viruses...)
	•	Physical hazards (human hair, fingernails, wooden equipment, plastic utensils,...)
	•	Chemical hazards (sanitisers, pesticides, fertilisers, toxic metals...)
	•	Allergenic hazards (allergic to nuts, shellfish, dairy, sesame,...)
184. Why is temperature important when making bread dough?
The final temperature of a dough after mixing indicates the dough has been mixed enough to a point where the yeasts are developed. The ideal temperature is between 24°C and 26°C.
	•	Too low temperature: the dough won't rise well, 
	•	Too hight emperature : the dough will over-ferment.
185. What is temperature for crusty bread? (low or high? long time or short time?)
The highest oven setting 240°C  – 250°C   for the first 10 minutes and then 220°C  or 200°C   (depending on how brown you want your bread crust to be)

-salmonella 
-why: Because harmful bacteria grow quickly at this temperature, increasing the risk of food poisoning. Keeping food in the danger zone for too long can cause digestive illnesses. Therefore, it is important to store food in the correct place and temperature in the right time.

17. Store chemicals.
Where to store chemicals:
• In cool, dry places away from food.
• Separate chemicals that can react with each other.
• In a locked area for safety.
• Ở nơi mát, khô, xa thực phẩm.
• Lưu trữ riêng biệt các hóa chất có thể phản ứng với nhau.
• Khu vực có khóa để đảm bảo an toàn.
How to store chemicals:
• Follow the label instructions.
• Use labeled containers.
• Store on strong shelves, at eye level or lower.
• Keep chemicals out of reach of children and pets.
• Tuân theo hướng dẫn trên nhãn.
• Sử dụng bao bì được ghi nhãn rõ ràng.
• Lưu trữ trên kệ chắc chắn, ở tầm mắt hoặc thấp hơn.
• Để xa tầm với của trẻ em và động vật.
Free hand technique
“Free hand technique means cooking without measuring exactly, just using your feeling and experience to make the dish taste right.”

17. what is standard recipe card?
A Standard Recipe Card (SRC) is a document that provides all the necessary details to make a dish consistent. It includes ingredients, cooking steps, portion size, and serving instructions.
Standard Recipe Card (SRC) là một tài liệu cung cấp tất cả thông tin cần thiết để chế biến một món ăn một cách nhất quán. Nó bao gồm nguyên liệu, các bước nấu, kích cỡ phần ăn và hướng dẫn phục vụ.
Tại sao phải follow SRC? 
-Ensure the dish is always consistency.
-Control the quality of the dish
-Avoid waste and control food costs.
-Saves time in preparation.
-Makes it easy to train new staff.

- to control the size of food, and ensure that the quality is not too much or too little.

	•	Using crockery, tools, equipment. 
	•	Learn different vegetables and herbs. Cutting techniques. 
	•	Making simple dishes: salads, dressings, sauces, stocks, side dishes.
	•	Making more complex dishes: steaks, seafood dishes, grill, stew, baked products. 

	•	What is Par Level (Par Stock level)?
	•	Amount of stock to run the business. 
	•	2 levels: Minimum and maximum.
	•	inventory check 
	•	What happens if there is too much stock?
	•	High wastage (waste), low quality of stock because of the long storing time. 
	•	Not enough space for storage. 
	•	What methods are used to control par stock? 
	•	Write the correct orders; (Determine the correct quantity.)
	•	Check the stock every day. 
	•	Check the Use By Date before storing; Follow FIFO (First-in, First out: we use item stored first).
	•	Temperature of fridge (0-5oC), freezer (-18oC), danger zone (5-60oC), reheating food (75oC inside/in the center), keeping food hot (over 60oC)?
	•	 How do you cool hot food? (The principle of cooling food)
	•	Divide into small portions.
	•	Use shallow containers, stir evenly.
	•	Cool to 21oC within 2 hours.
	•	Then, chill in the fridge and cool to 5oC within 4 hours. 
	•	What is contamination? 
	•	Physical, chemical, biological agents
	•	& Allergens contact with food. 
Cross-contamination? (Transfer of contaminants from one item to other items)
	•	 What are uses of different chopping boards? (What are colour-coded chopping boards)
	•	In my kitchen, we use 6 different chopping boards:
	•	Red: for raw meat
	•	Yellow: for raw poultry
	•	Blue: for raw fish and seafood
	•	White: for dairy products and pastry
	•	Green: for fruit and vegetable
	•	Brown: for cook food.
	•	If you want to cut vegetable and cooked meat in the same chopping board, what should you do?
	•	Actually, I never use one chopping board for 2 ingredients. 
	•	If there is only 1 chopping board in the kitchen, what should you do?
	•	I will report to manager or restaurant owner. 
	•	 Do you use chopping boards that have bad smell?
	•	Throw away/ Discard them. (I never use).
	•	 How do you know the amount of food you need to prepare?
	•	Based on the number of customer’s orders, standard recipe cards, portion control, and par stock levels.
	•	 What is SRC? What information is shown?
	•	Standard Recipe Card: name of dish, ingredients, amount of ingredients, cooking method and steps, portion, tools and equipment, picture. 
	•	What are different heating methods? (Phương pháp truyền nhiệt)
3: Conduction (solid-metal/flat grill); convection (liquid-water, stock, oil and air); Radiation (hot ray)
	•	What is convection? => It is a heating method where heat is transferred by liquid or air. 

	•	 How to record, monitor and control the wastage (lãng phí, dư thừa)?
	•	Follow FIFO, check the use by date, label carefully.
	•	Store correctly.
	•	Calculate the yield test carefully. 
	•	Use the edible trimmings for making stocks, sauces, salads, fillings. 
	•	 What is FIFO?
	•	 What is 2-hour/4-hour rule?
Food in the danger zone:
	•	Within 2 hours: we can use or store.
	•	From 2 to 4 hours: we can use immediately.
	•	More than 4 hours: we need to throw away. 
	•	 How do you receive fresh chicken? How do you store it?
	•	Check paperwork;
	•	Check temperature of fresh chicken. It should be 0-5oC.
	•	Check quality and quantity, such as brand, colour, smell, size, weight, use by date, label and packaging. 
	•	If everything is ok, move to cool room or fridge to store. 
	•	Sign the document. 
	•	 Which temperature do you cook poultry to?
	•	Over 75oC in the center/middle. 
	•	 How do you crumb chicken? How do you fry crumbed chicken?
	•	Cut into right size. Keep it dry, season with salt and pepper.
	•	Cover with all-purpose flour.
	•	Dip into beaten egg.
	•	Cover with breadcrumbs. Put onto a tray. 

	•	Use oil at 2/3 of the deep fryer.
	•	Set temperature at 160-180oC.  
	•	Place crumbed chicken in hot oil using frying basket, deep fry until golden brown colour. 
	•	Who is responsible for keeping health and safety of the kitchen? 
	•	Every staff member. 
	•	 When using spray cleaning chemical, what PPE (Personal Protective Equipment) should you wear?
	•	Gloves, mask, safety boots. 
	•	What do you use to clean equipment and tools? 
	•	Chemicals: Detergents, Sanitizers or hot water.
	•	Towels, sponge, abrasives, brushes, mop, broom, dustpan, vacuum machine. 
	•	 How do you clean the kitchen bench? What do you use to clean?
	•	Remove dirt, food scraps and oil. 
	•	Use wet towel with detergent to clean the kitchen bench.
	•	Use clean wet towel to wipe again.
	•	Use sanitizer to kill bacteria.
	•	Let it dry.
	•	What do you do for pest control? (How do you control pests - Harmful animals)
	•	Keep kitchen tidy and well-organized.
	•	Cover well and store everything on the shelves, fridge or cool room. 
	•	Remove/handle garbage several times a shift. 
	•	Clean the garbage bin every day, put lid on. 
	•	Use fly screen, traps to protect food from pests.
	•	Call for pest controller regularly. 

	•	What do you do when machine is broken?
	•	Alarm other workers, and make a note. 
	•	Remove electrical plug. 
	•	Report to the leader, manager.
	•	Arrange for repair. 

	•	Why do you need to know about kitchen hazards?
	•	To protect ourselves and other staff.
(Eliminate any risks to the worker’s safety and health.)
	•	To prevent accidents.
	•	To ensure to follow laws properly.
	•	If food gets chemical contamination, what will you do?
	•	Throw away = discard. 
	•	 How do you carry a knife when walking in the kitchen?
	•	Alarm everybody.
	•	Keep close to the leg, tip down to the floor. 
	•	How do you hold the knife to cut? 
(Do you have a good knife skill? – Good enough)
	•	With Knife holding hand: my fingers hold the handle firmly.
	•	Free hand: hold ingredient, fingertips curve backward. 
	•	What do you think about when making roster? (Nâng cao, cho chef)
	•	The number of customers.
	•	The occasion and the shift. 
	•	The ability of staff. 
	•	The convenience and desire of staff member. 
	•	Family responsibility. 
	•	The workplace regulations and labour law.
	•	How do you put/place/arrange stocks in the fridge/ cool room? (Shelving)
	•	Cakes, read-to-eat food on the top shelf.
	•	Canned food, dairy products, and prepared food on the middle shelves. 
	•	Raw meat, poultry, seafood and fish putting on containers or trays on the bottom shelf. 
	•	Vegetables and fruits in the box.  (Why? => to prevent soil, insects and chemicals in the vegetable contact with other food)
	•	How often do you have to defrost (rã đông) freezer (tủ đông)? 
	•	Once a month. = twice a month. 
	•	Every 2 months. 
	•	 Why should you put raw meat at the bottom of the fridge?
	•	Avoid meat juice dripping into other food. 
	•	 What should you do if food fells on the floor? 
	•	Throw away, (because food can be contaminated by physical, chemical and biological agents.)
	•	 If items in the stock or coolroom are expired (out of date), what should you do? 
	•	Throw away. 
	•	Report to the manager, record on the stock document. 
	•	Find solutions/recommendation to avoid: 
	•	place correct orders.
	•	follow FIFO more strictly; 
	•	check the label every day; 
	•	If the items are nearly out of date, offer some promotions.
	•	 How do you defrost food? 
	•	Defrost food in the fridge overnight;
	•	If in rush/hurry, defrost under running water, change water regularly;
Or: Use microwave oven. 
	•	 How often do you change oil in deep fryer (Nồi chiên)? (when)
	•	I will change oil when it has dark colour and bad smell. 
hoặc: In my kitchen, I often change oil after 2-4 days depending on the speed of use. 
Câu hỏi liên quan: If during the service time, you see the oil is dark, what do you do?
	•	In my kitchen, there are 2 deep fryers. If one deep fryer is dark, I will use another one. 
	•	What do you do with the used oil? 
	•	Collect the oil for recycling. 
	•	 How do you maintain the cleaning and safety at kitchen? 
	•	Always keep personal Hygiene and workplace hygiene in the kitchen;
	•	Follow HACCP and FIFO.
	•	Control Pest.
	•	Maintain equipment and machine. 
	•	Use the PPE appropriately. 
	•	Which standard about food safety do you follow in your country/in Vietnam?
	•	In Vietnam, we follow Food Safety Act 2010. Besides, Western-style restaurants also apply HACCP system.
(HACCP is a international system to ensure food safety.)
	•	How do you lift a heavy item?
	•	I’ll sit down, bend my knees, keep back straight, and slowly lift the item. 
	•	If it’s too heavy, I’ll ask for help or use lifting machine and trolley. 
	•	 If oil spills on the floor, what should you do?
	•	Alarm other people, place the safety sign. 
	•	Cover flour on the spill, use paper to remove it.

	•	Fish and seafood
	•	Gluten products
	•	Honey, alcohol.
	•	Chemicals; flowers, insect bites.

	•	Good preparation
	•	Prepare & Follow Mise en Place. I prepare all ingredients and tools before the service time. 
	•	Follow the instructions, SRC strictly. 
	•	 If customer has complaint, what do you do? (Nên kể 1 câu chuyện có thật)
In this case, I will 
	•	Listen to the customer’s complaint.
	•	Say sorry to them.
	•	Confirm the problem. 
	•	Ask them how to make it up to them.
	•	Take action, follow up and report to the manager.
	•	 How many types of salad are there? How many types of dressings?
2 types of salads: 
	•	simple salads (one main ingredient): green salads, tomato salads, potato salads, cucumber salad. 
	•	Mix salads: couscous salads, Nicoise salads, 
How many types of dressings? 
	4 basic types: Mayonnaise, Mayonnaise-based; vinaigrette and vinaigrette-based
	•	Vinaigrette: mix 3 parts of oil : 1 part of vinegar, salt and pepper.
	•	Mayonnaise: egg York, mustard, sugar + vinegar; => oil slowly in. 
	•	
	•	What elements to look at when making salads? 
	•	Fresh ingredients cut into bite-size
	•	Dressings before serve. 
	•	Eye-catching, colour contrast is applied.
	•	Garnish must be edible and suitable for the salad.
	•	4 Parts of a salad: 
	•	Base: salad greens, vegetable.
	•	Body: main ingredients such as meat, chicken, beef, seafood, fish, eggs and cheese.
	•	Garnish: red radish, croutons, bacon, capsicum, cold cut, herbs.

	•	 Tell me some salads that you have made? (Kể tên)
	•	Green salads (Vinaigrette), tomato salads (Vinaigrette, mayonnaise, olive oil), Nicoise salad, Greek salads.

	•	Fruit salad: avocado, dragon fruit, mango, strawberries, pear/apple, pomelo, blue berries, mayonnaise/ yoghurt
	•	Caesar salad: Romain lettuce, bacon, grilled /roasted prawn or chicken, parmesan cheese, croutons, tomato, Caesar dressing (mayonnaise, (egg York), anchovy, parmesan cheese, carper, garlic, salt and pepper)

	•	Nicoise salad: sauteed tuna, green, red onion, cucumber, green and red pepper, tomato; vinaigrette dressing (3 olive oil : 1 part of vinegar; pepper, salt, honey, egg York, mustard)
	•	How do you make Ceasar salad? 
	•	Romain lettuce (cut bite-size); chopped bacon, roasted chicken, croutons, parmesan cheese powder; Caesar dressing including mayonnaise, lemon juice, caper, anchovy, shallot, garlic, salt and pepper.  
	•	What bread is used for sandwich?
	•	Types of bread: White bread, Wholegrain bread, Cornmeal bread, sourdough, hot dog, Baguette, flat bread - tortillas, Naan, Chapati.
	•	What are basic types of sandwiches? 
	•	Conventional sandwich: 2 slices of bread, spreads (mayonnaise, butter, cream cheese) fillings, sauces. 
	•	Open sandwich: 1 slice of bread, spread, fillings, toppings, sauces.
	•	Finger sandwich/tea sandwich: 2 slices of bread, soft filling (cream, mayonnaise, butter, cheese, chop vegetables, chop bacon, chop boil egg), wrapped and chilled before cut. 
	•	Pinwheel sandwich: 1 big slice of flat bread, soft fillings, rolled, wrapped, chilled before cut. 
	•	Club sandwich: similar to conventional sandwich, with more than 2 slices of bread, cut half into triangle (diagonally), secured by a wooden stick. 
	•	Pocket sandwich: baguette, spreads, fillings, sauces, vegetables.
*What sandwiches do you make? 
	•	 What is a stock? Why is a stock quality important?
	•	It’s a flavouring water/liquid, made from bones, vegetables and herbs.
	•	It’s used as a base to make many other dishes such as soups, sauces, stews and braises.
	•	How many types of stocks? 
	•	White stocks: white beef stock, chicken stock, fish stock, vegetable stock.
	•	Brown stocks: brown beef stock. 
	•	 How do you make beef stock (white and brown), chicken stock, fish stock?
	•	White stocks: 
	•	I Blanch beef/chicken/fish bones. 
	•	Put everything into the stock pot with the ratio 10 parts of cold water to 5 parts of bones to 1 part of vegetables and herbs.
	•	Simmer in 6-8 hours (for beef stocks); 2-4 hours (for chicken stock); 20 minutes (for fish stock). 
	•	Brown beef stock: 
	•	Roast the beef bones until brown.
	•	Put 10 parts of cold water, 5 parts of roast bones, 1 part of vegetables and herbs.
	•	Simmer in 6-8 hours. 
	•	Fish stock: bland fish bones => Put 10 parts of cold water, 5 parts of fish bones, 1 part of vegetables and herbs. => simmer in 20 minutes (why do you simmer fish stock in 20 mins? => to avoid the stock becoming bitter)
	•	How should stock be? 
Good quality stock: no fat, no cloudy, be clear, full of flavour, have a gelatinous nature. 
	•	 What is Glace?
	•	What is Demi-Glace?
	•	 What is thickening agent?  What are used as thickening agents? 
	•	A ingredient helps liquids to be thickened. 
	•	Cornflour (corn starch), roux, gelatine, agar-agar, gums. 
	•	 What are basic types of soups?
	•	Thin soups: clear soup (Con.so.me), broth soup
	•	Thick soups: Cream soup, puree soup, chowder, Bisque soup (seafood, shellfish, fish stock) 
	•	Cold soups: tomato cold soup, Gazpacho. 
	•	National soups: Pho, Miso soup, Tomyum, French Onion soup, Minestrone, Gazpacho. 
	•	What is Roux? (white, yellow, brown, dark brown)
	•	A thickening agent, often used for soup and sauces, made from flour and butter, ratio 1:1 (1 to 1)
	•	How many mother sauces are there? 5
	•	Bechamel: white roux and milk
	•	Veloute: white roux and white stocks (chicken stock, fish stock, white beef stock)
	•	Espagnole: brown roux and brown beef stock. 
(Glace/ Demi-glace: reduce brown beef stock to 1/10th. / Reduce brown beef stock to a half.)
	•	Hollandaise (Warm-emulsion sauce): 
	•	reduce white wine vinegar with bay leaf, peppercorn, onion.
	•	add the reduction to the egg york. Whisk well. 
	•	add clarified butter slowly to the egg york mixture over a steam/ hot water.
	•	If you pour too much oil into the egg york mixture, it will be separate. 
	•	 How to fix: 1st way: add some drops of boiling water to the edge of the bowl and whisk well. 2nd way: mix another mixture of egg york and Dijon mustard and add into the separate Hollandaise, whisk well. 
	•	Mayonnaise (Cold-emulsion sauce): mix egg York with vinegar or lemon juice, whisk well. Slowly add olive oil into the mixture, whisk well in one way. Season with salt and pepper.
	•	Tomato sauce: cook tomato, mirepoix, garlic, bay leaves, basil. Cook until soft and blend. 
	•	What is an emulsion? 
	•	This is an agent that helps incorporate 2 unmixable ingredients. 
	•	Example: vinegar and oil can’t be mixed together. Adding egg York will make these ingredients incorporated. Egg York is an emulsifier. 
	•	 How many degrees of doneness for red meat (beef, pork, veal, lamb, goat)?
	•	Blue: like raw meat, temperature under 50oC. 
	•	Rare: temperature at 52oC, outside light brown, inside, red, still have blood. 
	•	Medium rare: temperature at 55oC, outside brown, inside bright pink. 
	•	Medium: temperature at 63oC, outside brown, inside warm pink.
	•	Medium well: temperature at 66oC, outside dark brown, inside light pink.
	•	Well done: temperature at 71oC, outside dark brown, inside light brown, no pink. 
	•	How do you check the doneness of steak?
	•	Look at the steak visual.
	•	Touch the meat / check the tenderness. 
	•	Check temperature by using thermometer. 
	•	 How do you choose fresh beef, lamb, pork, chicken? 
	•	Right temperature. 
	•	Look at the visual & smell. 
	•	Touch for the tenderness. 
	•	Check label, packaging & use-by-date. 
	•	Beef: bright cherry red, creamy fat. Firm when touch. 
	•	Lamb: rosy pink, white fat. 
	•	Pork: light pink, white and soft fat. 
	•	Chicken: bright light yellow, firm, elastic skin without bruises, bright flesh, tight bones and joints. 
	•	What is HACCP?
	•	Hazards Analysis Critical Control Point.
	•	Is an international system for controlling food safety. 
*What procedures are included in HACCP?
HACCP includes procedures to receive kitchen supplies and food, check, store, keep personal hygiene, keep workplace hygiene, control pest, clean and sanitize, maintain equipment, and so on.
*Do you follow HACCP in your restaurant?
- In my kitchen, we also follow HACCP. 
- In my kitchen, we don’t follow HACCP. But, we must follow Vietnamese Food Safety Act 2010.
	•	If the good is at 5oC when receiving, should you put it outside or put in the cool room?
	•	I will check other quality elements such as use-by-date, smell, packaging, colour.
	•	I’ll immediately move to the cool room. 
	•	 If the good is at 7oC when receiving, should you put it outside or put in the cool room?
	•	Refer to my workplace procedure to receive goods. 
	•	Or I’ll ask for my manager’s opinion. 
	•	Why do you check the invoice when getting supplies?
	•	To ensure the correct quantity and quality such as temperature, use-by-date, and how to store the supplies.
